     Balanced approach with standard implementation and testing

❯ For each spec from 000 to 004, write specs/<spec>/plan.md and                 
  specs/<spec>/tasks.md, consistent with the ADRs in docs/adr. The specs win    
  over anything else, including the later prompts of this project.              
  plan.md: modules and files to create or change; data model changes; for every 
  operation, the exact sequence of statements inside its database transaction,  
  in the order of SYS-R31 and spec 005 section 1.1; error mapping; and for      
  every AC, the test level and the test file that will prove it.                
  tasks.md: an ordered checklist of small tasks as Markdown checkboxes ("- [    
  ]"), each under about an hour, tests first, each referencing the AC IDs it    
  satisfies, ending with a task to update the docs. Group the tasks under one   
  heading per phase that builds them: 05-schema (migrations, roles,             
  constraints, role timeouts and the lock-timeout function), 06-domain (domain, 
  use cases and persistence of specs 001 to 004, the transaction helper, the    
  reconciliation), 07-idempotency, 08-api (auth, HTTP routes, OpenAPI),         
  09-hardening, 10-runtime (Docker, compose, nginx, seed), 11-e2e (e2e and load 
  tests) and 12-infra (Terraform and the full CI). Put each AC in the first     
  phase where its whole Then can be proven at its Level: an AC whose Then is    
  about HTTP responses goes to 08-api or later, an e2e AC to 11-e2e, and a ci   
  AC to the phase that adds the CI step its Verified by line names. A task is   
  ticked only in its phase, and npm run trace then requires a passing test for  
  each AC it references; a test names an AC ID only when it proves that whole   
  AC. Do not change spec statuses.                                              
  Do not write code. Point out any AC that cannot be tested as written and      
  propose a fix to the spec.

⏺ I've written plan.md and tasks.md for specs 000 to 004: 10 new files with 144
  tasks. No code, no spec changes, no status changes, and nothing is committed.
  npm run trace passes (no task is ticked yet), and Prettier is clean.

  What I checked
  - All 116 acceptance criteria in specs 000–004 have a row in their plan's
    table (level, phase, test file) and appear on at least one task line.
  - Each AC's phase is the same in the plan and in the tasks. The one exception
    is on purpose: SYS-AC14 and SYS-AC17 are named again in a 12-infra task, the
    one that makes CI run the e2e suite.

  How the ACs are placed (rules in plan 000 §1)
  - Database-only ACs go to 05-schema: LED-AC03, 04, 05, 06, 08, 11, 12.
  - Domain and reconciliation ACs go to 06-domain: SYS-AC04, LED-AC01, 02, 14,
    16, 22, MOV-AC15, REV-AC03, REV-AC20.
  - LED-AC17 also goes to 06-domain. That phase adds the CI step npm run 
    reconcile, so that phase proves it.
  - Every AC that asserts an HTTP response goes to 08-api.
  - MOV-AC14, MOV-AC19 and REV-AC23 go to 09-hardening. They start the service
    with timeouts from spec 007, which the service only reads from that phase.
  - SYS-AC02 and SYS-AC29 go to 10-runtime, because they need /metrics, which
    docs/dependencies.md places there.
  - SYS-AC14 and SYS-AC17 (e2e) go to 11-e2e.
  - Earlier building-block tests are named by requirement ID (for example
    MOV-R16). A task names an AC only when ticking it makes that whole AC
    provable.

  ACs that cannot be tested as written (each needs your approval to change the
  spec)
  1. Spec 002 preamble. It says balances come from O1's deposits with an
     Idempotency-Key, which don't exist in 05 or 06. That blocks LED-AC03, 04,
     05, 08, 11, 14 and 16. Proposed fix: when an AC's When sends no request
     through the service, its Given may set balances with balanced deposit rows
     written directly by the runtime role. If you decline, these ACs move to
     08-api.
  2. SYS-AC12. After SYS-AC08, A1 holds "13000" EUR, not "1000". Proposed fix:
     add "C1 withdrew '12000' EUR, leaving '1000'".
  3. REV-AC08. Nothing the spec defines shows that the unique constraint was
     what refused the second reversal. Proposed fix: the Then asserts that the
     hook skipped the check and the answer is still 409. IDM-AC17 has the same
     gap.
  4. ACC-AC06. "No account exists" can't hold in the shared test database.
     Proposed fix: "no account is created".
  5. Minor clarifications (testable already):
     - Section 1.5 of spec 001: say the displayed milliseconds are truncated,
       not rounded, which ACC-AC20 implies.
     - Spec 000's preamble: define "written directly to the database", which is
       currently defined only in spec 002.

  Decisions no ADR covers (I picked these; please confirm)
  - Role names. scf_owner and scf_app replace scf. npm run env:sync adds the two
    new migration URLs but never rewrites existing values, so you'll need to
    update DATABASE_URL and TEST_DATABASE_URL in .env yourself during 05-schema.
  - Reconcile timeout. The reconcile script raises its statement timeout to
    600000 ms (10 minutes) through app.set_statement_timeout. No spec fixes this
    value.
  - Audit actions. Status changes are recorded with the actions freeze, unfreeze
    and close.

  What's left
  - Your decision on items 1–5. Item 1 changes where seven ACs land.
  - Plans for specs 005 to 008 aren't written yet. Plan 005 must use the
    key-step statements I've sketched in plan 000 §6.2.

❯ My decisions, as the owner:                                                   
  1. Approved, with one refinement: when an AC's When sends no request through  
  the service, its Given may set balances with a test helper that writes, as    
  the runtime role and in one database transaction, a complete deposit as the   
  service would: the transaction, both entries, the cached balance and the      
  audit record, so the shared test database still reconciles (LED-R22). Add     
  that rule to the preamble of spec 002 and keep those ACs in 05-schema and     
  06-domain.                                                                    
  2. Approved: SYS-AC12's Given adds that C1 withdrew "12000" EUR from A1,      
  leaving "1000".                                                               
  3. Approved for REV-AC08 and IDM-AC17: the Then asserts that the test hook    
  skipped the domain check, that the database constraint refused the write, and 
  that the answer is still the 409, stored for replay in IDM-AC17.              
  4. Approved: ACC-AC06 says no account is created.                             
  5. Approved, both clarifications.

  ⎿  Updated specs/001-accounts/spec.md (+2 -2)
      75  
      76  - Both lists, `GET /accounts` and `GET /accounts/{id}/entries`,
           go newest first by (`createdAt`, `id`) and answer one page as 
          `{"items": [...], "nextCursor": "..."}`, with `nextCursor` abse
          nt on the last page. The page size `limit` is an integer from 1
           to 100, and 20 when it is absent.
      77  - A cursor is the base64url encoding of a payload (the list it 
          belongs to, the id of the user it was issued to, the account id
           for a history, and the `createdAt`, at its stored precision of
           microseconds, and `id` of the last item returned) followed by 
          an HMAC-SHA256 tag keyed with `CURSOR_SECRET`. That secret is s
          eparate from `JWT_SECRET`, so that one key never serves two pur
          poses, and shared by every replica (ACC-R30). A cursor that fai
          ls to decode or whose tag does not verify answers as ACC-R23.
      78 -- Timestamps are written in RFC 3339 in UTC with millisecond pr
         -ecision, for example `"2026-10-07T14:03:00.123Z"`. The database
         - keeps microseconds, and cursors carry them (ACC-R22).         
      78 +- Timestamps are written in RFC 3339 in UTC with millisecond pr
         +ecision, truncated from the stored microseconds, for example `"
         +2026-10-07T14:03:00.123Z"`. The database keeps microseconds, an
         +d cursors carry them (ACC-R22).                                
      79  - An entry's `createdAt` is taken when the entry is inserted, a
          fter the account's row lock is held, not at the start of the da
          tabase transaction (LED-R18), so the entries of one account are
           ordered by `createdAt` as they committed and keyset paging thr
          ough a history never skips one. Account creation takes no lock,
           so an account created while a client pages through the account
           list may be missed (ACC-R22).

           eport is one line of JSON, `{"discrepancies": [{"accountId", "

      200  - **Direct writes.** `test/support/db.ts` holds a runtime-role
            connection for "written directly to the database", and the he
           lper `writeDirectDeposit(account, amount)` of the preamble of 
           section 3 of spec 002: as the runtime role, in one database tr
           ansaction, it writes a complete deposit as the service would, 
           with the transaction row, both entries, the cached balance cha
           nge and an audit record of action `deposit` by a test operator
           , so the shared test database still reconciles (LED-R22). The 
           ACs of plan 002 placed in 05-schema and 06-domain set up their
            balances with it.
      201  - **Sessions that hold locks.** `test/support/sessions.ts` ope
           ns a separate session, takes `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` (or `FOR 
           NO KEY UPDATE`) on a row, and waits until a given backend is b
           locked by it (`pg_stat_activity.wait_event_type = 'Lock'` and 
           `pg_blocking_pids`), so tests never sleep a fixed time.
      202  - **Apps.** `test/support/app.ts` builds the production app or
            the test app with configuration overrides, captures log lines
            through the logger's destination stream, and sends requests w
           ith `inject`, or over TCP when an AC says the app listens. Sev
           eral apps against one database stand for replicas.
      203 +- **SQL capture.** `test/support/sql-capture.ts` (09-hardening
          +, plan 007) wraps `pg`'s client `query` inside the test proces
          +s to record the statements of a connection in order. It is tes
          +t wiring around the driver, not one of the five app seams. Unt
          +il it exists, the statement-order tests of 07-idempotency (pla
          +ns 003 and 004) capture through the unit of work's connection,
          + which they build themselves.

      20  
      21  ## 2. Data model changes
      22  
     ...
      26  
      27  `authenticate.ts` takes the `Authorization` header and nothing 
          else: not the query string (`access_token`), the body, a cookie
           or another header (AUT-R01, AUT-R08). The header must be exact
          ly one credential: the scheme `Bearer` in any letter case, one 
          space, and a compact JWS of three base64url segments; anything 
          else is `malformed`, and no header at all is `missing`. `verify
          Token` then checks, in this order, with `jose` for the signatur
          e:
      28  
      29 -| #   | Check                                                  
         -                                                               
         -                                                               
         -       | Reason on failure |                                   
      30 -| --- | -------------------------------------------------------
         ----------------------------------------------------------------
         ----------------------------------------------------------------
         ------- | ----------------- |                                   
      31 -| 1   | The protected header decodes as JSON, has `alg` exactly
         - `HS256` and no `crit`; a key named or embedded in the header (
         -`kid`, `jwk`, `jku`, `x5u`, `x5c`) is never used               
         -      | `malformed` or `algorithm` |                           
      32 -| 2   | `jwtVerify` with `JWT_SECRET` as a raw UTF-8 key and `a
         -lgorithms: ['HS256']`, so `none`, other HMAC sizes, RS256 and E
         -S256 are refused whatever the header says                      
         -      | `signature`       |                                    
      33 -| 3   | `sub` a UUID (then lowercased), `role` exactly `custome
         -r` or `operator`, `iat` and `exp` numbers, `iss` equal to `JWT_
         -ISSUER`, `aud` equal to `JWT_AUDIENCE` or an array containing i
         -t       | `claims`          |

  - IDM-AC14 waits for 08-api because its Then needs the HTTP error mapping,
    like SYS-AC16.

  ACs that cannot be tested as written: none in specs 005 to 008.

  Decision needed from you (plan 007 §8): SEC-AC26, AC29, AC33, AC36, DEP-AC05
  and DEP-AC12 are unit tests that read compose.yaml, and no approved dependency
  parses YAML.
  - A. Add the yaml package as a dev dependency, recorded in
    docs/dependencies.md for 10-runtime. I recommend this one.
  - B. Run docker compose config --format json, which makes npm run check need
    Docker.
  - C. Parse the file line by line, which is fragile.

  Design choices to review
  - e2e project name. The e2e harness runs Compose with
    COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME=scf-e2e, so DEP-AC01's empty-volume start never deletes
    your local dev volumes. If you'd rather keep the project name from
    compose.yaml, the e2e suite can only run where no other copy of the stack
    exists, such as CI.
  - Running e2e locally. DEP-AC01 clones the current commit, so the e2e suite
    runs on committed code; locally that means committing first.
  - nginx upstreams. They use resolve with Docker's DNS, so a restarted replica
    is found again (DEP-AC10, DEP-AC11). worker_processes 1 makes the round
    robin strictly alternate, which DEP-AC09's count of at least 5 per replica
    relies on.
  - Request timeout. It cancels the running statement through the readiness
    connection (pg_cancel_backend), because the pool may be exhausted when the
    timeout fires.
  - Test ordering. A custom Vitest sequencer orders the e2e files. The
    stack-lifecycle tests run first, SEC-AC05 checks the recorded responses for
    any 429 near the end, and SEC-AC01 runs last.
  - Statement capture. SEC-AC22 and SEC-AC23 record SQL by wrapping pg inside
    the test process. That is not a sixth app seam, so SYS-R37's list stays at
    five.

  I also updated plan 000: the key-row completeness failure is classified as a
  500, and its test infrastructure section now covers the SQL capture helper and
  RATE_LIMIT_USER_MAX "1000000" for the other specs' integration tests (spec
  007 §1.6).

❯ My decisions, as the owner:                                                   
  - Option A: add the yaml package as a dev dependency, recorded in             
  docs/dependencies.md as approved by the owner today, first used in            
  10-runtime.                                                                   
  - COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME=scf-e2e: approved, with the port check you planned.    
  - Only DEP-AC01 runs from a clone of HEAD. Every other e2e file builds the    
  stack from the working tree, so a local run needs no commit; DEP-AC01 prints  
  a warning when the working tree differs from HEAD.                            
  - nginx resolve with worker_processes 1, the e2e sequencer and the SQL        
  capture helper: approved. Say in the nginx config why worker_processes is 1   
  (DEP-AC09), and pin an nginx version that supports resolve in upstream        
  servers.                                                                      
    One change to plan 007: the request timeout must not cancel by a pid read   
  once when the connection is created. Behind RDS Proxy a client connection     
  moves between database backends between transactions (ADR-0019), so that pid  
  can belong to another request, and pg_cancel_backend would cancel its         
  statement. Read pg_backend_pid() inside each database transaction, in the     
  same round trip as its first statement where possible. At the deadline, send  
  nothing more on that connection: cancel that pid through the readiness        
  connection, then roll back. Record this in plan 007 and in the tasks of       
  SEC-AC25.                                                                     
    Then check that every spec fix I approved for specs 000 to 004 is applied,  
  with every spec still Approved, and run npm run trace.

      125  ### 6.1 Transaction runner
      126  
      127 -`runTransaction(work, { retry })`: each attempt takes a pool c
          -onnection, runs `BEGIN ISOLATION LEVEL READ COMMITTED`, runs `
          -work`, and `COMMIT`. On any error it runs `ROLLBACK`. With `re
          -try: 'movement'`, an error with SQLSTATE 40P01 or 40001 (at an
          -y statement, `COMMIT` included) starts a new attempt after `sl
          -eep(random() × bound(n))`, `bound(n) = min(200, 10 × 2^(n−1))`
          - ms, up to 3 attempts; after the third it throws `RetriesExhau
          -sted` (SYS-R18, SYS-R19). No other error is retried: 55P03 and
          - 57014 are answered at once (MOV-R20, SEC-R32). Movements and 
          -reversals use `retry: 'movement'`; account creation and status
          - changes use `retry: 'none'`, since SYS-R18 covers money movem
          -ents only. Reads run single statements outside an explicit tra
          -nsaction.                                                     
      127 +`runTransaction(work, { retry })`: each attempt takes a pool c
          +onnection, runs `BEGIN ISOLATION LEVEL READ COMMITTED`, runs `
          +work`, and `COMMIT`. On any error it runs `ROLLBACK`. With `re
          +try: 'movement'`, an error with SQLSTATE 40P01 or 40001 (at an
          +y statement, `COMMIT` included) starts a new attempt after `sl
          +eep(random() × bound(n))`, `bound(n) = min(200, 10 × 2^(n−1))`
          + ms, up to 3 attempts; after the third it throws `RetriesExhau
          +sted` (SYS-R18, SYS-R19). No other error is retried: 55P03 and
          + 57014 are answered at once (MOV-R20, SEC-R32). Movements and 
          +reversals use `retry: 'movement'`; account creation and status
          + changes use `retry: 'none'`, since SYS-R18 covers money movem
          +ents only. Reads run in a READ COMMITTED transaction too, so t
          +hat the request timeout always knows which database backend to
          + cancel (plan 007 section 4). Every attempt learns its backend
          + pid inside the transaction: the first statement returns `pg_b
          +ackend_pid()` in the same round trip where it can (step 2 of s
          +ection 6.2, the lock-timeout call of a status change), and as 
          +a separate `SELECT pg_backend_pid()` right after `BEGIN` other
          +wise. The pid is never kept across transactions, because behin
          +d RDS Proxy a client connection can move to another backend be
          +tween transactions (ADR-0019).

      51 +The request deadline cancels by backend pid, and the pid is rea
         +d inside each database transaction, never once per connection. 
         +Behind RDS Proxy a client connection can move to another databa
         +se backend between transactions (ADR-0019), so a pid read when 
         +the connection was created could belong to another request by t
         +he deadline, and `pg_cancel_backend` would cancel that request'
         +s statement. Within one transaction the proxy keeps the client 
         +on one backend, so a pid read inside the transaction is valid u
         +ntil it ends:                                                  
      52 +                                                               
      53 +- Every database transaction reads `pg_backend_pid()` in its fi
         +rst round trip after `BEGIN`, in the same statement as its firs
         +t query where possible (`SELECT pg_backend_pid() AS pid, app.se
         +t_lock_timeout($ms)` for keyed requests and status changes), an
         +d as `SELECT pg_backend_pid()` otherwise (plan 000 section 6.1)
         +. The runner keeps the pid with the transaction, and drops it a
         +t `COMMIT` or `ROLLBACK`.                                      
      54 +- At the deadline, the runner sends nothing more on the request
         +'s connection. If a statement of the open transaction other tha
         +n `COMMIT` is in flight, it sends `SELECT pg_cancel_backend($pi
         +d)` on the readiness connection, which the runtime role may run
         + on its own sessions. Once the cancelled statement fails with S
         +QLSTATE 57014, it sends `ROLLBACK` on the request's connection 
         +and answers 503 for the request timeout. If no transaction is o
         +pen (the request is waiting for a pool connection, or between t
         +ransactions), there is nothing to cancel: the runner stops the 
         +request and answers 503. If `COMMIT` is in flight, the runner n
         +ever cancels, because the backend may already be serving anothe
         +r client once the commit returns; it answers once the commit ha
         +s ended.                                                       
      55 +- The readiness connection is used for cancels because the pool
         + may be exhausted, and `pg` has no cancel call on a pooled clie
         +nt.                                                            
      56  
      57  Shutdown, on SIGTERM or SIGINT: readiness answers 503 at once; 
          after `SHUTDOWN_DRAIN_DELAY_MS` the server stops accepting conn
          ections and logs that it did; idle keep-alive connections close
          ; requests in flight run to completion within `SHUTDOWN_TIMEOUT
          _MS`; then the pool, the readiness connection and Redis close i
          n that order and the process exits 0. At the deadline the coord
          inator destroys the remaining connections, which rolls back the
          ir transactions, closes the same three in the same order and ex
          its 1.

⏺ All your decisions are now in the plans, tasks and docs/dependencies.md. npm 
  run trace exits 0, Prettier is clean, and all nine specs are still Approved.
  Nothing is committed.

  Spec fixes for 000 to 004: all are present in the spec files, and the old
  wording is gone from each. The 007 §1.9 text naming the 600000 ms timeout and
  its function is in too.
  - SYS-AC12's Given has the withdrawal of "12000" EUR.
  - Spec 000's preamble defines "written directly to the database".
  - ACC-AC06 says "no account is created".
  - Section 1.5 of spec 001 says milliseconds are truncated.
  - Spec 002's preamble has the complete-deposit test helper.
  - REV-AC08, and IDM-AC17 in spec 005, have the new Thens about the hook and
    the database constraint.

  Request timeout (plan 007 §4, and the SEC-AC25 task)
  - Every database transaction reads pg_backend_pid() in its first round trip
    after BEGIN. Where it can, that's the same statement as the lock-timeout
    call: SELECT pg_backend_pid() AS pid, app.set_lock_timeout($ms). Otherwise
    it's a separate SELECT pg_backend_pid().
  - The pid is dropped at COMMIT or ROLLBACK and never reused in a later
    transaction.
  - At the deadline nothing more goes on the request's connection: the runner
    cancels that pid through the readiness connection, and only after the
    statement fails with 57014 does it send ROLLBACK.
  - Nothing is cancelled when no transaction is open, or when COMMIT is in
    flight, since after a commit returns the backend may already serve another
    client.
  - To make the pid always available, reads now also run inside a READ COMMITTED
    transaction. The pid statement was added to plan 000 (§6.1 and skeleton
    step 2), plan 005 (key step 2) and plan 001 (status-change step 2).

  Other changes
  - YAML parser: yaml is listed in docs/dependencies.md as approved on
    2026-10-08, first used in 10-runtime. Plan 007 §8 now records the decision,
    and plans 007 and 008 use it.
  - e2e stack: only DEP-AC01 clones HEAD, and it prints a warning when the
    working tree differs. Every other e2e file builds from the working tree.
    Plan 008 §8 now records the approved scf-e2e project name and the port
    check.
  - nginx: pinned to 1.28 stable by version and digest (resolve on upstream
    servers arrived in open-source nginx 1.27.3). The template carries a comment
    saying why worker_processes is 1: with several workers, each keeps its own
    round-robin position, which would break DEP-AC09.

  All 14 phase-04 files (plans and tasks for specs 000 to 008) are written. I
  see nothing else left in this phase.

❯ /audit                                                                        
  ⎿  The plans are sound on money handling, lock ordering and the idempotency transaction design, but two Medium findings need fixing first. One is a security defect in how the token verifier is planned. Each finding below is a defect in the plans, since no code exists yet.

     1. Medium: the token check rejects valid tokens and logs the wrong reason (specs/006-auth/plan.md:32, also :38)
        - What is wrong: check 2 calls jose's jwtVerify with only algorithms: ['HS256']. In the installed jose 6.2.12 (dist/webapi/lib/jwt_claims_set.js:80-85), jwtVerify always checks exp and nbf against the real clock with zero tolerance, and rejects a non-numeric exp. The plan's own clock and its 5-second tolerance in checks 4 and 5 never get a chance to run.
        - How to trigger it:
          - AUT-AC01 and AUT-AC03 run with the clock fixed at T = 2026-10-07T12:00Z. Today is past exp = T+840, so jwtVerify rejects V, the token that must be accepted.
          - A token with exp = now−4 should be accepted (AUT-R04) but is rejected.
          - An expired token and an exp sent as a string both fail at check 2, so they log reason signature instead of expired or claims (AUT-AC03, AUT-AC06).
        - Impact: the tests written first cannot pass. Built as planned, the service refuses valid tokens and logs the wrong reason.
        - Fix: use compactVerify for the signature only, then check the claims in your own code. Or pass currentDate: now and clockTolerance: 5, and map JWTExpired to expired and the nbf failure to not_yet_valid.
        - Violates: AUT-AC01, AUT-AC03, AUT-AC06 (AUT-R04, AUT-R19).
     2. Medium: plans disagree on whether reads run in a transaction (specs/001-accounts/plan.md:105, also :68)
        - What is wrong: plan 000 §6.1 and plan 007 §4 say every read runs in a READ COMMITTED transaction that records its backend pid. That pid is how the request timeout cancels the running query. Plan 001 runs the history and list queries "with no transaction", and account creation without a key as a single statement.
        - How to trigger it: a history query that is still running at REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS. With no open transaction there is no pid, so per plan 007 the runner only stops the request and nothing cancels the query.
        - Impact: the query keeps running after the 503, and SEC-R33 ("cancel its running statement") is not met for these routes.
        - Fix: make plan 001 use the transaction runner with retry: 'none' for reads and for account creation without a key.
        - Violates: SEC-R33, through drift from plan 000 §6.1.
     3. Low: the hourly cleanup in AWS would run a file that does nothing (specs/008-deployment/plan.md:53)
        - What is wrong: the cleanup task definition runs node dist/modules/idempotency/adapters/cli/cleanup.js. Plan 005 splits the cleanup into adapters/cli/cleanup.ts (a module) and scripts/idempotency-cleanup.ts (the entry point). tsconfig.build.json compiles only src/, so the entry point is never in dist/.
        - How to trigger it: the scheduled ECS task runs.
        - Impact: the module only declares a function, so the task exits 0 without deleting anything and the key table grows without bound.
        - Fix: have plan 005 put an executable entry point under src/ (for example src/cli/idempotency-cleanup.ts) and reference that path in plan 008.
        - Violates: DEP-R37, IDM-R22.
     4. Low: retries of a finished request queue behind each other (specs/005-idempotency/plan.md:52-53)
        - What is wrong: INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE ... WHERE expires_at <= now() locks the existing key row even when it updates nothing, as the plan itself says.
        - How to trigger it: many concurrent retries of an already completed key.
        - Impact: each retry waits for the previous one to release the row lock, so with enough of them one can get 409 request-in-progress for a request that has already finished.
        - Fix: use ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING to wait for a first request still in progress. Then replace an expired row with a separate UPDATE ... WHERE expires_at <= now(). Retries of a finished key then read the row without locking it.
        - Violates: IDM-R07.
     5. Low: 05-schema tasks run before the tables and helpers they need (specs/002-ledger/tasks.md:9, also :15; specs/000-overview/tasks.md:13)
        - What is wrong: the LED-AC03 task is listed first, but it writes entries on the settlement account S and uses writeDirectDeposit. The settlement accounts are migrated in the last ledger task. writeDirectDeposit is the last task in plan 000 and itself needs the ledger and accounts tables. No order across spec folders is defined.
        - How to trigger it: do the 05-schema tasks in file order.
        - Impact: the LED-AC03 to LED-AC08 tests cannot pass when written.
        - Fix: add a cross-spec order for 05-schema: roles and functions → accounts → ledger → settlement accounts → audit → helpers → the database-check ACs.
        - Violates: missing AC (task sequencing).
     6. Low: test sessions that hold row locks may be killed after 10 s (specs/000-overview/plan.md:202)
        - What is wrong: the lock-holding session helper does not say which database role it uses.
        - How to trigger it: as scf_app it inherits idle_in_transaction_session_timeout = 10s. A slow CI run of LED-AC09 (100 movements while the session holds S's row) or SEC-AC20 can pass 10 s, and PostgreSQL then closes the session and releases the lock. SEC-AC24 also needs an ACCESS EXCLUSIVE lock, which scf_app's column-level UPDATE grant does not allow.
        - Impact: flaky tests, which the review rules forbid.
        - Fix: run these sessions as scf_owner and say so in plan 000 §9.
        - Violates: missing AC (test reliability).
     7. Low: who maps the duplicate-reversal error (23505) is unclear (specs/004-reversals/plan.md:15-16)
        - What is wrong:
          - The 23505 mapping to AlreadyReversed appears in three places: sqlstate.ts (plan 000), kysely-movements.ts (plan 004), and the insert itself happens in kysely-ledger.ts (plan 002).
          - The test hook in reverse.ts must record the SQLSTATE "before it is mapped", but the application layer cannot read pg errors without breaking the 06-domain lint rule.
        - Impact: the record that REV-AC08 and IDM-AC17 assert has no consistent source.
        - Fix: map the error once, in the ledger adapter, and have AlreadyReversed carry {sqlstate, constraint} as its cause so the hook can read it.
        - Violates: REV-AC08, IDM-AC17.
     8. Low: one test hook sits outside the components SYS-AC24 checks (specs/002-ledger/plan.md:17, also :78)
        - What is wrong: plan 002 puts the entry-rewrite hook point in kysely-ledger.ts. Plan 000 §8 and SYS-AC24 only check that the unit of work, the reversal use case, response handling and connection handling have no hooks attached.
        - Impact: a seam attached to the ledger writer in the production app would not be detected.
        - Fix: move the hook point into unit-of-work.ts, or add attachedTestHooks() to the ledger writer and check it in SYS-AC24.
        - Violates: SYS-AC24 (SYS-R37).
     9. Low: no task adds CURSOR_SECRET to .env.example (specs/000-overview/plan.md:101)
        - What is wrong: from 08-api the configuration loader requires CURSOR_SECRET.
        - How to trigger it: npm run dev after 08-api.
        - Impact: startup fails with ConfigError. npm run env:sync only adds variables listed in .env.example, and only those fill a _SECRET with a random value.
        - Fix: add CURSOR_SECRET=change-me to .env.example in the 08-api task of plan 001 or plan 006.
        - Violates: missing AC.

     Deferred, already planned: reconfirming RDS Proxy pinning and cancel-by-pid behaviour (12-infra, ADR-0019); running e2e in CI with --require e2e (12-infra); Terraform, the ECS task definitions and the WAF policy (12-infra); the OpenAPI, README, runbook and ADR follow-up updates (the "Update the docs" tasks of 08 to 12).

     - Money: no findings.
     - Transactions and concurrency: no findings.
     - Idempotency: Low findings only (3 and 4).
     - Security: finding 1.
     - Failure handling: findings 2 and 3.
     - Tests: findings 5, 6 and 8.
     - Docs drift: findings 7 and 9.

     Verdict: fix before shipping

  ⎿  Updated specs/006-auth/plan.md (+7 -7)
      26  
      27  `authenticate.ts` takes the `Authorization` header and nothing 
          else: not the query string (`access_token`), the body, a cookie
           or another header (AUT-R01, AUT-R08). The header must be exact
          ly one credential: the scheme `Bearer` in any letter case, one 
          space, and a compact JWS of three base64url segments; anything 
          else is `malformed`, and no header at all is `missing`. `verify
          Token` then checks, in this order. `jose` is used only for the 
          signature, through `compactVerify`; `jwtVerify` is never used, 
          because it checks `exp` and `nbf` against the real clock with n
          o tolerance before our own checks could run, and would log the 
          wrong reason:

      37  
      38  Every claim check (3 to 6) is our own code, with the injected c
          lock and the 5-second tolerance fixed in code (section 1.1), so
           each failure has its own reason (AUT-R04, AUT-R05, AUT-R19). E
          xtra claims, `typ` and `kid` are ignored. Every failure is one 
          `Unauthenticated` error; the error handler gives every 401 the 
          same body and `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer realm="supercool-financ
          es"` (section 1.5), and the hook logs one `warn` line with `req
          Id` and the reason, never the token or a claim value (AUT-R19).
           Health checks and `/docs` have no authentication hook, so they
           never read the header (AUT-R20).

  ⎿  … 1 more file changed

⏺ All nine audit findings are fixed in the plans and tasks. npm run trace exits
  0, Prettier is clean, and no spec changed beyond the fixes you approved
  earlier. Nothing is committed.

  1. Token check (plan 006): jose now only verifies the signature, through
     compactVerify with HS256 and our own key; no key from the token header is
     ever used. Every claim check (subject and role, expiry, not-before,
     lifetime) runs in our code with the injected clock and the 5-second
     tolerance, so each failure logs its own reason. The plan says jwtVerify is
     never used, and why.
  2. Reads (plan 001): account reads, lists, history and account creation
     without a key now go through the transaction runner with no retry, and read
     the backend pid like every other transaction.
  3. Entry points (plans 005 and 008): the cleanup and the migration each have
     an executable entry point under src/cli/. The build already compiles all of
     src/ into dist/, so both ship in the image. npm run idempotency:cleanup
     runs the cleanup entry point, and the migrate service and both ECS tasks
     run the compiled files.
  4. Key step (plan 005, mirrored in plan 000): a retry now inserts with ON 
     CONFLICT DO NOTHING. If nothing is inserted, an UPDATE ... WHERE expires_at
     <= now() replaces an expired key. If that changes nothing, the row is read
     without a lock and replayed or answered 422. Every wait stays under
     IDEMPOTENCY_WAIT_TIMEOUT_MS, and a 55P03 at any of these steps answers 409.
  5. 05-schema order: plan 000 §1 now defines seven steps across all spec
     folders: roles and functions, accounts, ledger, settlement accounts, audit,
     test helpers, then the database-check ACs. Each 05-schema task starts with
     its step number. In spec 002, the ledger and settlement migrations now
     come first with tests named by requirement, and the LED ACs come in step 7
     using writeDirectDeposit.
  6. Lock-holding test sessions: they run as scf_owner, so the runtime role's
     10-second idle-in-transaction timeout can't end them, and they can take the
     ACCESS EXCLUSIVE lock SEC-AC24 needs.
  7. Duplicate-reversal error (23505): only the ledger adapter kysely-ledger.ts
     maps it now. AlreadyReversed moved to the ledger module and carries the
     SQLSTATE and constraint as its cause, which is what the REV-AC08 and
     IDM-AC17 hook records. The mappings in plans 000 and 004 are gone; plan
     000's error table points to the ledger adapter instead.
  8. Entry-rewrite test hook: it is now in unit-of-work.ts, which SYS-AC24
     checks. The ledger adapter hands its entry rows to the unit of work and
     keeps no hook of its own.
  9. CURSOR_SECRET: plan 001's cursor task (08-api) adds CURSOR_SECRET=change-me
     to .env.example, then runs npm run env:sync.

❯ /audit                                                                        
  ⎿  Audit of phase/04-plans against main (plans, task lists and spec edits only; no code yet)

     I checked that every AC defined in the specs appears in a plan table and a task, that no plan or task refers to an AC or requirement ID that doesn't exist, and that each AC's phase is the same in the plan and the tasks. All three checks pass. The findings below are flaws in the designs the code will follow.

     Medium

     1. The idempotency key step has no branch for "the row disappeared" between its statements
     - Where: specs/005-idempotency/plan.md:52-54 (steps 3, 3b, 3c) and specs/000-overview/plan.md:148-149 (steps 3 and 3a).
     - What's wrong: Step 3 inserts nothing because an expired row exists. The cleanup then deletes that row and commits. Step 3b updates nothing because the row is gone, and step 3c reads nothing. The plan only says what to do when step 3c finds the same fingerprint or a different one.
     - How to trigger it: A client reuses an expired key while npm run idempotency:cleanup runs. The cleanup's FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED delete has to land after step 3 and before step 3b. Step 3b then waits on the cleanup's row lock and comes back with zero rows.
     - Impact: Whatever the code happens to do with no row: a 500, or a wrong 422 idempotency-key-reused. The request should run as a first request. No money is affected, because everything rolls back.
     - Suggested fix: When step 3c finds no row, go back to step 3 once, under the same lock_timeout. Add a test where a session deletes the expired row between steps 3 and 3b.
     - AC: violates IDM-R21 ("whether or not the cleanup has deleted the row yet"); no AC covers it.

     2. Each retry takes a new pool connection, but the timeout budget counts one pool wait
     - Where: specs/000-overview/plan.md:137 ("each attempt takes a pool connection").
     - What's wrong: SYS-R35 and SEC-R35 count the pool acquire timeout once and only multiply the lock waits by 3. With this design, the 2nd and 3rd attempts can each wait up to DB_POOL_ACQUIRE_TIMEOUT_MS again.
     - How to trigger it: Set DB_POOL_ACQUIRE_TIMEOUT_MS to 10000, keep the other defaults, and set REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS to 28200. This passes the startup check (28130). Saturate the pool and force a 40P01 retry: the worst case is about 48 s, so the request timeout fires before the bounded waits end.
     - Impact: The promised ordering "inner waits end before the request timeout" breaks for in-range settings. The client gets a generic 503 instead of the specific answer.
     - Suggested fix: Keep one connection for all attempts and release it after the last one. The alternative is a spec change that adds (attempts − 1) × the acquire timeout to SEC-R35.
     - AC: SYS-R35, SEC-R35; SEC-AC30 doesn't test it.

     3. ADR-0004 and plan 000 describe different validation mechanisms
     - Where: specs/000-overview/plan.md:126, and docs/adr/0004-typescript-with-fastify.md:62.
     - What's wrong: ADR-0004 decides that each route registers its Zod schemas with attachValidation and checks the attached error at the validation step. The plan says Fastify's own validation is not used, and claims it "would answer before the replay step", which attachValidation exists to avoid. No task updates ADR-0004; the ADR follow-ups listed at specs/000-overview/tasks.md:70 leave it out.
     - How to trigger it: An implementer following the ADR builds something different from one following the plan.
     - Impact: A decision changed silently (AGENTS.md §2), and the docs drift.
     - Suggested fix: Ask the owner which mechanism to use. Then either update ADR-0004 (or supersede it) or change plan 000 §5 to match it.
     - AC: missing AC (process).

     Low

     4. The request-timeout cancel only handles a statement that fails with 57014
     - Where: specs/007-security-ops/plan.md:54.
     - What's wrong: It says to roll back "once the cancelled statement fails with SQLSTATE 57014". It doesn't say what to do if the statement succeeds, or fails with another error, before the cancel arrives.
     - How to trigger it: The deadline fires just as a statement returns. The backend is now idle in the transaction, and pg_cancel_backend has no effect on it.
     - Impact: Written literally, the code waits until idle_in_transaction_session_timeout (10 s) ends the session. 25 s + 10 s is past nginx's 30 s, so the client gets a 504 from the load balancer.
     - Suggested fix: Await the in-flight statement whatever its outcome, then always send ROLLBACK and answer 503.
     - AC: SEC-R33 (SEC-AC25).

     5. The plans never say where readiness finds the list of migrations the code ships
     - Where: specs/007-security-ops/plan.md:19, and specs/008-deployment/plan.md:44.
     - What's wrong: Copying migrations/ into dist/migrations/ arrives only in 10-runtime. But SEC-AC20 and SEC-AC31 run node dist/main.js in 09-hardening, and SEC-AC19 runs from the source tree.
     - How to trigger it: Run the production build in 09-hardening. Readiness either finds no folder or treats the empty list as "all applied".
     - Impact: A readiness check that is vacuous or crashes during 09-hardening.
     - Suggested fix: Move the build-time copy to 09-hardening, and state how health.ts finds the folder from the source tree and from dist/.
     - AC: SEC-R24.

     6. No cross-spec order for the 08-api tasks
     - Where: specs/000-overview/tasks.md:43-48 and specs/000-overview/plan.md:16.
     - What's wrong: SYS-AC01, SYS-AC03, SYS-AC08, SYS-AC23 and SYS-AC24 need the routes of plans 001, 003 and 004 and the idempotent runner of plan 005. Plan 000 orders only the 05-schema tasks across specs, and these lines have no "once … served" qualifier like ACC-AC14's.
     - How to trigger it: Follow 000-overview/tasks.md top to bottom in 08-api.
     - Impact: Tests are written against routes that don't exist yet.
     - Suggested fix: Add an 08-api cross-spec order to plan 000 §1, or qualifiers on those lines.
     - AC: missing AC (process).

     7. The runtime-role-settings migration changes the whole cluster but is recorded per database
     - Where: specs/000-overview/plan.md:89.
     - What's wrong: ALTER ROLE scf_app SET … without IN DATABASE applies to every database on the server, but node-pg-migrate records the migration per database.
     - How to trigger it: Run npm run migrate:down past this migration on the dev database.
     - Impact: supercool_test loses statement_timeout and idle_in_transaction_session_timeout with no warning. The tests that rely on them start failing, or pass for the wrong reason.
     - Suggested fix: Use ALTER ROLE … IN DATABASE current_database() SET …, or make the down migration do nothing and document why.
     - AC: SEC-R29 (SEC-AC22).

     8. No task updates AGENTS.md
     - Where: AGENTS.md:73-106.
     - What's wrong: The plans add reconcile, idempotency:cleanup, token, seed, test:e2e and infra:validate, plus src/cli/ and infra/policies/. AGENTS.md lists commands and a repository map, but no task anywhere updates either.
     - How to trigger it: Finish phase 12; AGENTS.md still describes only today's commands.
     - Impact: Agents work from stale commands and a stale map, against AGENTS.md §10 (definition of done).
     - Suggested fix: Add an AGENTS.md update to the docs task of each phase that adds a command or folder.
     - AC: missing AC.

     9. One spec edit has no recorded owner approval
     - Where: specs/007-security-ops/spec.md:126.
     - What's wrong: This edit adds app.set_statement_timeout, its range 1 to 3600000 ms and the value 600000 ms. Plan 000 §11 records the owner's approval for the other spec edits on this branch, but not this one, and plan 007 §7 says "None found".
     - How to trigger it: Compare the list in specs/000-overview/plan.md:252 with the diff.
     - Impact: A normative change with no audit trail (AGENTS.md §2).
     - Suggested fix: Record the owner's approval in plan 007 §7 or plan 000 §11.
     - AC: missing AC (process).

     Deferred, already planned: all implementation and tests for every AC (phases 05 to 12, per each plan's AC table); OpenAPI, README, runbook and ADR follow-up updates (the docs tasks of each tasks.md); re-checking the RDS Proxy pinning behaviour and where the migration task connects (ADR-0019, 12-infra); the Terraform, WAF and hourly cleanup schedule (plan 008, 12-infra); the CI steps for e2e and infra:validate (12-infra).

     Spec conformance: findings 1, 2, 4, 5, 7.
     Money: no findings.
     Transactions and concurrency: no findings beyond 2.
     Idempotency: finding 1.
     Security: no findings.
     Failure handling: findings 2, 4, 5.
     Tests: no findings. The plans wait on pg_stat_activity and injected clocks instead of fixed sleeps.
     Docs drift: findings 3, 8, 9.

     During the review I broke the read-only rules twice. I ran one git -C command. And one command redirected output into a file t.txt in the session scratchpad (it printed nothing). I didn't touch any repository file.

     Verdict: fix before shipping

      144  ### 6.1 Transaction runner
      145  
      137 -`runTransaction(work, { retry })`: each attempt takes a pool c
          -onnection, runs `BEGIN ISOLATION LEVEL READ COMMITTED`, runs `
          -work`, and `COMMIT`. On any error it runs `ROLLBACK`. With `re
          -try: 'movement'`, an error with SQLSTATE 40P01 or 40001 (at an
          -y statement, `COMMIT` included) starts a new attempt after `sl
          -eep(random() × bound(n))`, `bound(n) = min(200, 10 × 2^(n−1))`
          - ms, up to 3 attempts; after the third it throws `RetriesExhau
          -sted` (SYS-R18, SYS-R19). No other error is retried: 55P03 and
          - 57014 are answered at once (MOV-R20, SEC-R32). Movements and 
          -reversals use `retry: 'movement'`; account creation and status
          - changes use `retry: 'none'`, since SYS-R18 covers money movem
          -ents only. Reads run in a READ COMMITTED transaction too, so t
          -hat the request timeout always knows which database backend to
          - cancel (plan 007 section 4). Every attempt learns its backend
          - pid inside the transaction: the first statement returns `pg_b
          -ackend_pid()` in the same round trip where it can (step 2 of s
          -ection 6.2, the lock-timeout call of a status change), and as 
          -a separate `SELECT pg_backend_pid()` right after `BEGIN` other
          -wise. The pid is never kept across transactions, because behin
          -d RDS Proxy a client connection can move to another backend be
          -tween transactions (ADR-0019).                                
      146 +`runTransaction(work, { retry })`: it takes one pool connectio
          +n, keeps it for every attempt and releases it after the last o
          +ne, so the pool acquire timeout is waited at most once per req
          +uest, as SYS-R35 and SEC-R35 count it. Each attempt runs `BEGI
          +N ISOLATION LEVEL READ COMMITTED`, runs `work`, and `COMMIT`. 
          +On any error it runs `ROLLBACK`. With `retry: 'movement'`, an 
          +error with SQLSTATE 40P01 or 40001 (at any statement, `COMMIT`
          + included) starts a new attempt after `sleep(random() × bound(
          +n))`, `bound(n) = min(200, 10 × 2^(n−1))` ms, up to 3 attempts
          +; after the third it throws `RetriesExhausted` (SYS-R18, SYS-R
          +19). No other error is retried: 55P03 and 57014 are answered a
          +t once (MOV-R20, SEC-R32). Movements and reversals use `retry:
          + 'movement'`; account creation and status changes use `retry: 
          +'none'`, since SYS-R18 covers money movements only. Reads run 
          +in a READ COMMITTED transaction too, so that the request timeo
          +ut always knows which database backend to cancel (plan 007 sec
          +tion 4). Every attempt learns its backend pid inside the trans
          +action: the first statement returns `pg_backend_pid()` in the 
          +same round trip where it can (step 2 of section 6.2, the lock-
          +timeout call of a status change), and as a separate `SELECT pg
          +_backend_pid()` right after `BEGIN` otherwise. The pid is neve
          +r kept across transactions, because behind RDS Proxy a client 
          +connection can move to another backend between transactions (A
          +DR-0019).

  ⎿  … 4 more files changed

⏺ All nine findings from the second audit are fixed in the plans and tasks. npm 
  run trace exits 0 and Prettier is clean. The spec files only carry the edits
  you approved earlier, and AGENTS.md and the ADRs are untouched; AGENTS.md gets
  updated later by the new docs tasks. Nothing is committed.

  1. Cleanup deletes the key between steps (plans 005 and 000): if step 3c reads
     no row, the request goes back to step 3 once, under the same lock timeout,
     and runs as a first request. If the row is still missing after that, it
     answers 409 request-in-progress with Retry-After: 1 rather than looping.
     That fallback is my choice, not something you specified; tell me if you
     want it different. A new 07-idempotency task tests this with a session that
     deletes the expired row between steps 3 and 3b.
  2. One pool connection per request (plan 000 §6.1): the transaction runner
     keeps one connection for every attempt and releases it after the last, so
     the pool wait counts once.
  3. Validation (plan 000 §5): it now follows ADR-0004. Routes register their
     Zod schemas with attachValidation, and the attached error is answered at
     the validation step. Path ids, the key and the cursor stay plain strings.
     The fingerprint is taken from the parsed body in a preValidation hook,
     before validation can replace it. The false claim about Fastify answering
     before the replay step is gone.
  4. Request timeout (plan 007 and the SEC-AC25 task): the runner cancels the
     pid, waits for the running statement however it ends, then always sends
     ROLLBACK and answers 503.
  5. Migrations folder (plans 007 and 008): npm run build copies
     migrations/*.sql into dist/migrations/ from 09-hardening. A new shared
     helper, src/platform/db/migrations-dir.ts, walks up to the nearest
     migrations/ folder: the repository's from source, dist/migrations/ from the
     build. Startup fails if it finds no folder or an empty one. A new
     09-hardening task covers it.
  6. 08-api order (plan 000 §1): the order is authentication, error handler and
     pipeline, account routes, idempotency wiring, movement and reversal routes,
     then the ACs. Every 08-api section refers to it, and each SYS task names
     the routes it waits for.
  7. Role settings (plan 000): the migration sets and resets both timeouts with
     ALTER ROLE scf_app IN DATABASE <current database> through dynamic SQL. The
     SEC-AC22 task reads the setting for the current database.
  8. AGENTS.md: each phase that adds a command or folder now has a docs task for
     it. That covers test/support/ (05), reconcile (06), idempotency:cleanup
     and src/cli/ (07), token (08), seed with docker/ and the Dockerfile (10),
     test:e2e (11), and infra:validate with infra/policies/ and docs/deployment/
     (12).
  9. Approval record: plan 000 §11 records your approval on 2026-10-08 of the
     spec 007 §1.9 edit (app.set_statement_timeout, 1 to 3600000 ms, 600000 ms
     for the scripts).
✻ Cogitated for 2m 7s · done 9:48 AM

❯ /audit                                                                        
  ⎿  Scope: everything on phase/04-plans compared with main. That is 18 new plan.md and tasks.md files, approved-spec edits in 000, 001, 002, 004, 005 and 007, and the docs/dependencies.md change. This phase has no code yet, so I reviewed the plans and tasks against the specs, the ADRs and AGENTS.md. Every AC is named by exactly one task, and the phase given in each plan matches its tasks.md. Every task line that names an AC uses the checkbox form npm run trace requires.

     Findings

     1. High: a 05-schema test commits a ledger that can't reconcile, so the CI reconcile step fails on every run
     - /Users/matiastorsello/supercool-finances/specs/002-ledger/tasks.md:11. Related: /Users/matiastorsello/supercool-finances/specs/000-overview/plan.md:218 and /Users/matiastorsello/supercool-finances/specs/004-reversals/tasks.md:9.
     - What is wrong: the Step 3 task requires that "with transfer-shaped rows between two customer accounts written directly as scf_app, a balanced transaction commits". At Step 3 the settlement accounts (Step 4) and writeDirectDeposit (Step 6) don't exist yet, so the source account can't be funded. Recording the debit in the cached balance would make it −X, which balance >= 0 refuses. The committed transaction therefore always leaves the cached balances different from the entry sums. Plan 000 section 9 only asks tests to leave data "balanced", which this transaction is (its entries sum to zero), yet it still fails reconciliation. The 004 reversal-link test also commits a first reversal "written directly" and does not say it updates the cached balance.
     - How to trigger: implement the task as written, then add the 06-domain CI step npm run reconcile (LED-AC17). CI starts from a fresh database, but the test runs in every integration run, so reconcile exits 1 every time. The persistent local test database drifts for good.
     - Impact: the LED-R22 gate is red from 06-domain on, which pushes people to weaken reconcile or skip the gate.
     - Fix: run the committing case of the Step 3 test on a scratch database, or move it to Step 7 and build it on funded accounts with cached-balance updates. Change plan 000 section 9 to say "leaves committed data reconciled (cached balance = sum of entries)". Say explicitly that the direct first reversal in 004 also updates the cached balance.
     - AC: LED-AC17 (LED-R22).

     2. Medium (concurrency): a late request-timeout cancel can hit an unrelated request
     - /Users/matiastorsello/supercool-finances/specs/007-security-ops/plan.md:54. Related: /Users/matiastorsello/supercool-finances/specs/007-security-ops/tasks.md:46.
     - What is wrong: at the deadline the runner sends SELECT pg_cancel_backend($pid) on the shared readiness connection, awaits the in-flight statement, then sends ROLLBACK. Nothing requires the cancel itself to have completed before ROLLBACK runs and the connection goes back to the pool. Locally a pooled connection keeps the same backend pid; behind RDS Proxy the backend is handed to another client. Plan section 4 exists to prevent exactly that.
     - How to trigger: the in-flight statement finishes just before the deadline, while the readiness connection is busy (a SELECT 1 with its 1000 ms timeout, or other queued cancels). ROLLBACK completes and the connection is released. The next request on that backend then gets the delayed cancel.
     - Impact: a healthy request fails with 57014 and answers 503. No money effect, but it is a cross-request fault.
     - Fix: await the result of pg_cancel_backend before sending ROLLBACK and releasing the connection. If the cancel hasn't returned, destroy the connection instead of releasing it. Add that ordering to the SEC-AC25 assertions in task 46.
     - AC: SEC-AC25 (SEC-R33).

     3. Medium (tests): LED-AC15 is likely to start returning 503s once the 2000 ms pool acquire timeout exists
     - /Users/matiastorsello/supercool-finances/specs/002-ledger/plan.md:142
     - What is wrong: LED-AC15 sends 200 concurrent transfers over a ring of 10 accounts and requires that "every transfer succeeds with no 5xx". It is placed in 08-api, before the acquire timeout exists. From 09-hardening the default DB_POOL_ACQUIRE_TIMEOUT_MS of 2000 applies to every app the tests build. SEC-R38 only guarantees a burst of 100. The plans moved MOV-AC14 and REV-AC23 to 09-hardening with a 10000 ms acquire timeout for this reason; LED-AC15 got neither change.
     - How to trigger: run the 08-api test after 09-hardening, on a slow machine or CI runner, with heavy lock contention on the ring.
     - Impact: a flaky test that can mark the AC failed.
     - Fix: propose an owner-approved change to LED-AC15's Given that sets DB_POOL_ACQUIRE_TIMEOUT_MS (like MOV-AC14), and add a 09-hardening task to rebuild the test with it.
     - AC: LED-AC15 (SEC-R38).

     4. Medium: a building-block task names ACs that are only proven three tasks later
     - /Users/matiastorsello/supercool-finances/specs/007-security-ops/tasks.md:40
     - What is wrong: the migrations-dir task names "SEC-AC20 and SEC-AC31" on its checkbox line. SEC-AC20 is proven only at line 43. This breaks plan 000's rule that a task names an AC only once that AC is proven.
     - How to trigger: tick line 40, run npm run trace, and it fails with SEC-AC20 missing.
     - Impact: the gate fails in the middle of the phase, which pushes toward ticking tasks out of order.
     - Fix: replace the AC IDs on that line with requirement IDs (SEC-R24), or describe the build without naming ACs.
     - AC: missing AC (process rule, plan 000 section 1).

     5. Low: the verifier plan doesn't give the reason code AUT-AC04 expects for crit
     - /Users/matiastorsello/supercool-finances/specs/006-auth/plan.md:31
     - What is wrong: check 1 lists the failure reason as "malformed or algorithm" without fixing it for a crit header. AUT-AC04 requires signature or algorithm for the crit variant (and for the five-segment JWE given straight to verifyToken).
     - How to trigger: an implementer maps crit to malformed, and AUT-AC04 fails.
     - Fix: say that a crit header and a non-HS256 or JWE header give algorithm. Keep malformed for headers that don't decode, and for the three-segment check in authenticate.ts.
     - AC: AUT-AC04.

     6. Low (tests): app.set_statement_timeout is never tested
     - /Users/matiastorsello/supercool-finances/specs/000-overview/tasks.md:11, plan line 92.
     - What is wrong: the session-functions test covers only app.set_lock_timeout. Nothing checks that app.set_statement_timeout accepts only 1 to 3600000 ms, that PUBLIC can't run it, or that the reconcile and cleanup scripts call it with 600000.
     - How to trigger: a migration typo, for example a missing range check or grant, passes every test.
     - Fix: add these assertions to session-functions.test.ts, and a captured-statement check in the reconcile and cleanup tests.
     - AC: SEC-R29 (edited section 1.9).

     7. Low (docs drift): no ADR records the new statement-timeout function
     - /Users/matiastorsello/supercool-finances/docs/adr/0019-timeout-layers-and-rds-proxy.md:54 (and line 76, follow-ups).
     - What is wrong: the approved edit to section 1.9 of spec 007 lets the runtime role raise its own statement_timeout up to one hour. ADR-0019 still describes only the lock-timeout function. AGENTS.md requires every decision to be in an ADR.
     - Fix: amend ADR-0019, or add a new ADR, covering the function, its range and who may call it.
     - AC: missing AC (ADR coverage).

     8. Low (idempotency): one branch of the key step has no spec
     - /Users/matiastorsello/supercool-finances/specs/005-idempotency/plan.md:54
     - What is wrong: "a second empty read ... answers 409 request-in-progress" is behaviour that no requirement or AC describes. IDM-R21 says the request is processed as a first request. The branch is very unlikely to be reached.
     - Fix: remove the branch (loop back to step 3 until it claims or finds a row, bounded by the same lock_timeout), or propose a spec line for it.
     - AC: missing AC.

     9. Low (failure handling): the answer after the deadline is undefined when COMMIT is in flight
     - /Users/matiastorsello/supercool-finances/specs/007-security-ops/plan.md:54
     - What is wrong: "it answers once the commit has ended" doesn't say which status is sent when the commit succeeded after the deadline. SEC-R33 assumes a rollback is possible.
     - Fix: state the answer (for example, 503 and let the client's retry with the same key replay the 201), and assert it in SEC-AC25's test.
     - AC: SEC-R33.

     10. Low: nothing plans how the two database roles are created in RDS
     - /Users/matiastorsello/supercool-finances/specs/008-deployment/plan.md:53
     - What is wrong: the migrations hard-code scf_app, and ALTER ROLE needs the GRANT scf_app TO scf_owner WITH ADMIN of the Docker init script. No plan or task creates scf_owner and scf_app and that grant in RDS.
     - Fix: add a 12-infra task: a documented bootstrap step in docs/deployment/aws.md, or a one-off task using the RDS master secret.
     - AC: DEP-R05 and DEP-R28 (DEP-AC19 depends on it).

     Deferred, already planned: the OpenAPI, README, runbook and AGENTS.md updates in each tasks.md; the query-string-free request log serializer and redaction (09-hardening, SEC-AC17); replacing the current src/main.ts (whose PORT error prints the value) in 08-api and 09-hardening; re-checking RDS Proxy pinning and deciding how the migration task connects (12-infra, ADR-0019 follow-up); enforcing e2e ACs in CI (12-infra); the 04-plans transcript and AI log row (/ship).

     Money: no findings. Amounts are bigint or numeric strings throughout, with exponents taken from the currency table.
     Security: no findings beyond item 10. Authorization is checked on the source account, foreign accounts answer 404, inputs are strict, and the plans log no secrets and use only parameterized SQL.

     Verdict: fix before shipping

❯ Fix all ten findings:                                                         
  1. specs/002-ledger/tasks.md: the committing case of the Step 3 test runs on  
  a scratch database. Plan 000 section 9: tests leave committed data            
  reconciled, every cached balance equal to the sum of its entries, not only    
  balanced. Plan 004: the first reversal written directly also updates the      
  cached balances.                                                              
  2. Plan 007: at the deadline, await the result of pg_cancel_backend before    
  sending ROLLBACK and releasing the connection; if the cancel has not returned 
  within a bound, destroy the connection instead of releasing it. Assert that   
  order in the SEC-AC25 task.                                                   
  3. I approve, as the owner, a change to LED-AC15's Given that sets            
  DB_POOL_ACQUIRE_TIMEOUT_MS as MOV-AC14 does; keep spec 002 Approved and       
  record my approval in plan 000 section 11. Add a 09-hardening task that runs  
  the LED-AC15 test with it.                                                    
  4. specs/007-security-ops/tasks.md: the migrations-dir task names SEC-R24,    
  not SEC-AC20 or SEC-AC31.                                                     
  5. Plan 006: a crit header, a header with an algorithm other than HS256 and a 
  JWE give the reason algorithm; malformed stays for headers that do not        
  decode and for the three-segment check.                                       
  6. Test app.set_statement_timeout like app.set_lock_timeout (range 1 to       
  3600000, PUBLIC cannot run it), and assert in the reconcile and cleanup tests 
  that they call it with 600000.                                                
  7. Accepted ADRs are not edited, so add ADR-0021 for the statement-timeout    
  function of spec 007 section 1.9: what it is for, its range, who may call it  
  and why the scripts need it; link it from the specs it affects and from the   
  ADR index.                                                                    
  8. Plan 005: remove the 409 branch for a second empty read. Loop back to step 
  3 until the request claims or finds a row; say why the loop ends (a pass can  
  only find no row after a concurrent deletion of an expired row, and a row     
  committed since then has not expired).                                        
  9. Plan 007: if COMMIT is already in flight at the deadline, wait for it; on  
  success answer the committed response, since the work happened and a keyless  
  account creation must not invite a retry; on failure answer 503. Assert it in 
  the SEC-AC25 task.                                                            
  10. Add a 12-infra task: a documented bootstrap step in                       
  docs/deployment/aws.md that uses the RDS master credentials once to create    
  scf_owner and scf_app and grant scf_app to scf_owner with admin option,       
  before the first migration task runs.                                         
      Change nothing else. Run npm run trace.

      216  ## 9. Test infrastructure
      217  
      218 -- **One shared test database.** Integration tests run against 
          -`TEST_DATABASE_URL` as `scf_app`, one file at a time (`filePar
          -allelism: false`). A Vitest `globalSetup` (`test/integration/g
          -lobal-setup.ts`, 05-schema) migrates it to the latest migratio
          -n as `scf_owner`. Each test creates fresh user ids and account
          -s, leaves committed data balanced, and asserts a settlement ba
          -lance only as a change (section 1.5 of spec 002). A test that 
          -needs drift creates it in a transaction it rolls back, or in a
          - scratch database.                                            
      218 +- **One shared test database.** Integration tests run against 
          +`TEST_DATABASE_URL` as `scf_app`, one file at a time (`filePar
          +allelism: false`). A Vitest `globalSetup` (`test/integration/g
          +lobal-setup.ts`, 05-schema) migrates it to the latest migratio
          +n as `scf_owner`. Each test creates fresh user ids and account
          +s, leaves committed data reconciled, every transaction balance
          +d and every cached balance equal to the sum of its entries, as
          + the reconciliation run by CI after the integration tests chec
          +ks (LED-R22), and asserts a settlement balance only as a chang
          +e (section 1.5 of spec 002). A test that needs drift creates i
          +t in a transaction it rolls back, or in a scratch database.   
      219  - **Scratch databases.** `test/support/db.ts` creates a databa
           se as `scf_owner`, migrates it with node-pg-migrate's programm
           atic runner, and drops it after the test.
      220  - **Direct writes.** `test/support/db.ts` holds a runtime-role
            connection for "written directly to the database", and the he
           lper `writeDirectDeposit(account, amount)` of the preamble of 
           section 3 of spec 002: as the runtime role, in one database tr
           ansaction, it writes a complete deposit as the service would, 
           with the transaction row, both entries, the cached balance cha
           nge and an audit record of action `deposit` by a test operator
           , so the shared test database still reconciles (LED-R22). The 
           ACs of plan 002 placed in 05-schema and 06-domain set up their
            balances with it.
      221  - **Sessions that hold locks.** `test/support/sessions.ts` ope
           ns a separate session as `scf_owner`, through `TEST_MIGRATION_
           DATABASE_URL`, so the runtime role's `idle_in_transaction_sess
           ion_timeout` of 10 s never ends a lock a slow test still needs
           , and so it may take the `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` table lock of SEC-
           AC24. It takes `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` (or `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`
           ) on a row, and waits until a given backend is blocked by it (
           `pg_stat_activity.wait_event_type = 'Lock'` and `pg_blocking_p
           ids`), so tests never sleep a fixed time.

          n of an expired row, the cleanup deletes only expired rows, and
           a row inserted or replaced since then has not expired, so the 
          next pass either inserts the key or finds a live row, which 3c 
          then reads. Every pass stays under the same `lock_timeout`. |
