⏺ All ten audit findings are fixed. npm run trace exits 0, Prettier is clean,
  and every spec is still Approved. Nothing is committed.

  1. Ledger test that broke reconciliation: the Step 3 schema test now commits
     its balanced transaction on a scratch database. Plan 000 §9 now requires
     tests to leave committed data reconciled, with every cached balance equal
     to the sum of its entries. The reversal-link task in 004 writes the
     original and the first reversal together with their cached balance changes.
  2. Late cancel (plan 007 §4 and the SEC-AC25 task): at the deadline, the
     runner waits for the result of pg_cancel_backend before it sends ROLLBACK
     and releases the connection. If the result isn't back within 1000 ms, it
     destroys the connection instead of returning it to the pool. The 1000 ms
     bound is my choice, since none was set; tell me if you want another value.
  3. LED-AC15: its Given in spec 002 now starts the service with
     DB_POOL_ACQUIRE_TIMEOUT_MS "10000", REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS "30000" and
     SHUTDOWN_TIMEOUT_MS "30000". MOV-AC14 sets all three, and the request
     timeout has to rise too, or the 10000 ms acquire timeout would fail the
     startup budget check. Your approval is recorded in plan 000 §11. Because
     its Given now uses spec 007 settings, LED-AC15 moved to a 09-hardening task
     in both the plan and the tasks.
  4. Migrations-folder task: it now names SEC-R24 instead of SEC-AC20 and
     SEC-AC31.
  5. Token reasons (plan 006): a crit header, an algorithm other than HS256
     (none included) and a JWE give algorithm. malformed is kept for headers
     that don't decode and for the three-segment check.
  6. app.set_statement_timeout tests: the session-functions test now covers it
     like the lock function: 600000 shows 10min, 0 and 3600001 are refused,
     3600000 is accepted, and PUBLIC can't run it. The LED-AC16 and IDM-AC24
     tasks assert from captured statements that the reconcile and cleanup
     scripts call it with 600000.
  7. ADR-0021, statement-timeout function for the maintenance scripts: it
     records the function's purpose, its range, that only scf_app may call it,
     and why the scripts need it. It weighs it against running the scripts as
     the owner, a third role, and SET LOCAL. It is linked from the Related ADRs
     of specs 002, 005 and 007 and from the ADR index. No accepted ADR was
     edited.
  8. Key step (plans 005 and 000): the 409 branch is gone. Finding no row loops
     back to step 3 until the request claims the key or finds a row. The loop
     ends because a pass can only come up empty after the cleanup deleted an
     expired row, and any row inserted or replaced since then has not expired.
  9. COMMIT in flight at the deadline (plan 007): the runner waits for the
     commit. It sends the committed response when the commit succeeds and 503
     when it fails, and the SEC-AC25 task asserts both.
  10. RDS bootstrap (plan 008 and a new 12-infra task): docs/deployment/aws.md
      will document a one-time step, run before the first migration task. It
      uses the RDS master credentials once to create scf_owner and scf_app and
      grant scf_app to scf_owner with admin option, the same as the local init
      script.

✻ Churned for 2m 54s · done 10:04 AM

❯ /audit                                                                        
  ⎿  Scope: the uncommitted phase 04-plans work on phase/04-plans. There are no commits ahead of main. That covers 18 new plan.md/tasks.md files, ADR-0021 and edits to 6 specs and 2 docs. No code changes in this phase, so the checks below apply to the designs the plans fix for later phases.

     Coverage: every AC in specs 000 to 008 appears in its plan's table and is named by a task. No task names an undefined AC. The phase in each plan table matches the tasks section, except where an AC is deliberately extended in 12-infra (SEC-AC29, SEC-AC36, SYS-AC14, SYS-AC17).

     Findings

     1. Medium: the key step can wait more than one idempotency wait per attempt

     - Where: specs/005-idempotency/plan.md:53-54,66 (and the same steps in specs/000-overview/plan.md:157-158).
     - What is wrong: the plan runs three statements under one lock_timeout set in step 2: step 3 (insert), step 3b (replace an expired row) and the 3c loop back to step 3. lock_timeout limits each lock wait, not their sum, so one attempt can wait for the key twice or more.
       - IDM-R12 says a key insert that does not complete within IDEMPOTENCY_WAIT_TIMEOUT_MS answers 409.
       - SYS-R35 and the SEC-R35 budget count exactly one idempotency wait per attempt.
     - How to trigger it:
       a. Key K has expired.
       b. R1 replaces K at step 3b and holds the row.
       c. R2 and R3 send K too and wait at step 3.
       d. R1 rolls back just before the 2000 ms limit (for example a 422 validation error).
       e. R3 takes the row lock at step 3b first.
       f. R2's step 3b now waits up to another 2000 ms.

       So R2 has waited about 4 s for the key before its own account locks. Add a transfer's two lock waits and 40P01 retries, and the worst case is 3 × (2 × 2000 + 2 × 2000) + 2100 = 26100 ms. That is above the default 25000 ms request timeout. With the boundary values the loader accepts (20131 ms), the margin disappears much sooner.
     - Impact: a request that should answer 409 request-in-progress can be cut off by the request timeout and answer 503 instead. That breaks the separate 409/503 semantics and the timeout budget the config check is meant to guarantee.
     - Suggested fix: take a deadline at step 2. Before step 3b and before every loop pass, call app.set_lock_timeout(remaining), and answer IdempotencyWaitTimeout when remaining is 0 or less. Add a building-block test for the 3 → 3b double wait to the 07-idempotency key-step.test.ts task.
     - AC violated: IDM-R12, SYS-R35 (budget of SEC-R35); no AC proves it (missing AC).

     2. Medium: the request-timeout protocol has no spec or ADR and departs from SEC-R33

     - Where: specs/007-security-ops/plan.md:54.
     - What is wrong: the plan defines the whole cancel protocol, and none of it is in spec 007 or ADR-0019:
       - backend pid read per transaction;
       - pg_cancel_backend sent through the readiness connection;
       - the connection destroyed if the cancel result takes more than 1000 ms;
       - a COMMIT already in flight is waited for and answered with the committed 201.

       SEC-R33 and ADR-0019 (line 54) both say a request past its timeout answers 503. AGENTS.md requires an AC for every behaviour and an ADR for every decision. The SEC-AC25 task asserts this extra behaviour under SEC-AC25's name, which does not describe it.
     - How to trigger it: a request whose COMMIT starts at about 24.9 s. statement_timeout (5 s) is the only limit on that COMMIT, so the answer can arrive around 30 s. That equals nginx's proxy_read_timeout.
       - The ordering database < service < load balancer (SEC-R34) breaks.
       - nginx can answer 504 for a committed movement.
       - A keyless account creation retried after that 504 creates a duplicate, which is exactly what this branch was meant to prevent.
       - The 1000 ms cancel wait also lets requests outlive REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS. Spec 007 line 122 assumes they cannot when SHUTDOWN_TIMEOUT_MS equals REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS.
     - Impact: behaviour that no spec describes, a contradiction with SEC-R33 and ADR-0019, and a broken timeout order in an edge case.
     - Suggested fix:
       - Propose a spec change to SEC-R33 for the commit-in-flight case, with its own AC.
       - Record the cancel mechanism in an ADR, or amend ADR-0019.
       - Bound the extra waits (commit and cancel) inside the budget: subtract them in SEC-R35, or keep REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS + 5 s + 1 s below the load balancer's timeout.
     - AC violated: SEC-R33, SEC-R34 (missing AC).

     3. Low: app.set_statement_timeout has no requirement or AC

     - Where: specs/007-security-ops/spec.md:126, specs/000-overview/tasks.md:11.
     - What is wrong: the approved spec edit adds the function only to the context prose of section 1.9, tagged SEC-R29. SEC-R29 is about the role settings, and SEC-R31 covers lock timeouts only. Its range (1 to 3600000), its transaction scope, who may execute it, and the scripts calling it with 600000 are tested under SEC-R31 and inside LED-AC16 and IDM-AC24, none of which state this behaviour.
     - How to trigger it: npm run trace cannot show that this behaviour is proven, and a regression would not fail any AC.
     - Impact: a security-relevant function that no AC proves.
     - Suggested fix: add an EARS requirement and an AC to spec 007 (owner approval needed), or extend SEC-R31 and SEC-AC23. Then name it in the 05-schema task.
     - AC violated: missing AC.

     4. Low: nothing automatically stops the service from raising its own statement timeout

     - Where: docs/adr/0021-statement-timeout-function-for-maintenance-scripts.md:30,70,76.
     - What is wrong: the service and the scripts share scf_app, so service code can call app.set_statement_timeout(3600000) and remove the 5 s safety net for that transaction. The ADR relies on code review only. SEC-AC23 checks for set_config( but not for this function.
     - How to trigger it: any service path, written by mistake, that calls the function.
     - Impact: one transaction can hold row locks for up to an hour.
     - Suggested fix: add a lint rule (no-restricted-syntax on that string), or a unit test, that allows the call only in ledger/adapters/cli/reconcile.ts and idempotency/adapters/cli/cleanup.ts. Add it to the 06-domain toolchain task.
     - AC violated: missing AC.

     5. Low: the 08-api order schedules four auth ACs before toProblem exists

     - Where: specs/000-overview/plan.md:28-29, specs/006-auth/tasks.md:23-27, specs/006-auth/plan.md:79.
     - What is wrong: step 1 (auth) comes before step 2 (toProblem, /v1 routes, the error handler). AUT-AC01, AUT-AC03, AUT-AC04 and AUT-AC05 assert "the error type that the HTTP edge maps to 401". The hooks.test.ts task needs the problem body and the /v1 routes. Plan 006 says toProblem "exists from 08-api", but within 08-api it comes later. Plans 000 and 005 already handle the same issue for SYS-AC16 and IDM-AC14 with an "extend once toProblem exists" task.
     - How to trigger it: following the tasks in order and ticking AUT-AC03 in step 1. Either its test does not prove the whole AC, or toProblem is pulled forward without a task.
     - Impact: an AC ticked before it is wholly proven, against the plans' own convention.
     - Suggested fix: in step 1, name these tests by requirement IDs (AUT-R03 to AUT-R05). Add a step 6 task that extends them to the AC IDs once toProblem exists, as SYS-AC16 does.
     - AC violated: AUT-AC01, AUT-AC03, AUT-AC04, AUT-AC05.

     Deferred, already planned: RDS Proxy pinning re-check (ADR-0019 and ADR-0021, 12-infra); e2e enforcement of SYS-AC14 and SYS-AC17 in CI (12-infra); Terraform extension of SEC-AC29 and SEC-AC36 (12-infra); the yaml dev dependency (10-runtime); OpenAPI, README, runbook and AGENTS.md command-table updates (in each plan's docs tasks); test/support/sql-capture.ts (09-hardening).

     Categories without findings

     - Money: no findings. Amounts are bigint in the domain and decimal strings at the API. Sums are numeric with no overflow, exponents come from the currency table, and lint bans number conversions in domain/ and application/.
     - Transactions and concurrency: nothing beyond finding 1.
       - One transaction per movement.
       - Customer accounts are locked one by one in ascending order, with kind = 'customer' on every lock.
       - System accounts are never updated or locked; the foreign-key FOR KEY SHARE is accepted.
       - Checks run after the locks.
       - Retries are bounded on 40P01 and 40001.
     - Idempotency: nothing beyond finding 1.
       - The key row is the first write.
       - A replay returns the stored status and bytes.
       - A different body answers 422.
       - Rejections are stored after rolling back to the savepoint; unexpected errors are not stored.
       - The key wait (409) and the account lock (503) are separate settings.
     - Security: nothing beyond finding 4. Authorization on the source account, 404 for foreign resources, strict schemas, parameterized SQL, and redaction are all planned.
     - Tests: no findings. Waits are observed through pg_stat_activity and pg_blocking_pids, with no fixed sleeps, and invariants are asserted as well as happy paths.
     - Docs drift: only the ADR-0019 conflict in finding 2. The ADR index, Related ADRs and docs/dependencies.md are consistent.

     Verdict: fix before shipping

❯ Fix all five findings. Finding 2 comes from my earlier instruction about      
  COMMIT in flight, which I withdraw.                                           
  1. Plans 005 and 000: take a deadline when the idempotency wait timeout is    
  set at step 2. Before step 3b and before every loop pass, call                
  app.set_lock_timeout with the time remaining, and answer                      
  IdempotencyWaitTimeout (409 request-in-progress) when none remains, so one    
  attempt never waits for the key longer than IDEMPOTENCY_WAIT_TIMEOUT_MS. Add  
  a building-block test of the 3 then 3b double wait to the 07-idempotency      
  key-step task.                                                                
  2. Plan 007, request timeout: at REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS the service answers 503   
  at once, as SEC-R33 says, and never waits for the database before answering.  
  The clean-up then runs after the answer: if a statement is in flight, await   
  pg_cancel_backend on the transaction's pid through the readiness connection   
  (destroy the connection if that call has not returned within 1000 ms), await  
  the statement, send ROLLBACK and release; if COMMIT is already in flight, it  
  cannot be rolled back: await it and release, and the answer stays 503, like   
  any response lost after a commit (IDM-AC21); keyed requests replay on retry.  
  Remove the commit-in-flight 201 branch. Record the mechanism (pid read per    
  transaction, cancel through the readiness connection, answer first and clean  
  up after, the commit-in-flight case) as a new ADR-0022, since accepted ADRs   
  are not edited, and link it from spec 007 and the ADR index. Make the         
  SEC-AC25 task assert only what SEC-AC25 says, and name the extra assertions   
  by SEC-R33.                                                                   
  3. I approve, as the owner, a new EARS requirement and AC in spec 007 for     
  app.set_statement_timeout: its range 1 to 3600000 ms, transaction-local,      
  executable only by the runtime role, and called with 600000 ms by the         
  reconcile and cleanup scripts only. Keep spec 007 Approved, record my         
  approval in plan 000 section 11, and name the new AC in the 05-schema task.   
  4. Add a unit test in the 06-domain toolchain task that fails when            
  app.set_statement_timeout appears anywhere in src/ outside the reconcile and  
  cleanup adapters.                                                             
  5. Plan 006 and its tasks: in 08-api step 1, name the verifier tests by       
  requirement IDs (AUT-R03 to AUT-R05); add a step 6 task that extends them to  
  AUT-AC01, AUT-AC03, AUT-AC04 and AUT-AC05 once toProblem exists, as SYS-AC16  
  does.                                                                         
     Change nothing else. Run npm run trace.

      63  - **404 at the lookup, or a business rejection** (409 `already-
          reversed`, the 422 types of section 1.3 of the spec), including
           one raised as a 23505 by the unique constraint of REV-R05: `RO
          LLBACK TO SAVEPOINT work`, step 9 with the problem response, `C
          OMMIT`.
      64  - **422 `validation-error`, a 503 (an account lock timeout, ret
          ries exhausted, a statement or request timeout) or a 500:** `RO
          LLBACK`, so the key row goes too and a retry runs again (IDM-R1
          6, IDM-R17). A 40P01 or 40001 re-runs the whole attempt from st
          ep 1, key insert included.
      65  
      66 -Steps 3, 3b and 3c all run under the `lock_timeout` of step 2, 
         -`IDEMPOTENCY_WAIT_TIMEOUT_MS` (IDM-R11), and a 55P03 at any of 
         -them is `IdempotencyWaitTimeout` (409, IDM-R12); one at any lat
         -er step is `AccountLockTimeout` (503, IDM-R13). `kysely-key-sto
         -re.ts` tells them apart by the step at which the error was rais
         -ed.                                                            
      66 +Steps 3, 3b and 3c share one wait budget: before step 3b and be
         +fore every pass back to step 3, the runner calls `SELECT app.se
         +t_lock_timeout($remaining)` with the whole milliseconds left un
         +til the deadline of step 2, and answers `IdempotencyWaitTimeout
         +` (409 `request-in-progress`, `Retry-After: 1`) at once when le
         +ss than 1 ms remains. `lock_timeout` bounds each wait, not thei
         +r sum, so without this an attempt could wait for the key once a
         +t step 3 and again at step 3b; with it, one attempt never waits
         + for the key longer than `IDEMPOTENCY_WAIT_TIMEOUT_MS` in total
         +, as SYS-R35 and SEC-R35 count it. A 55P03 at any of these step
         +s is `IdempotencyWaitTimeout` (409, IDM-R12); one at any later 
         +step is `AccountLockTimeout` (503, IDM-R13). `kysely-key-store.
         +ts` tells them apart by the step at which the error was raised.
      67  
      68  The replay is the stored `status`, `headers` and `body` bytes, 
          plus the current request's `X-Request-Id` and `Idempotent-Repla
          yed: true`. The replay step comes before validation and every l
          ater check, and the query string is not part of the fingerprint
          , so a replay never depends on `MAX_AMOUNT_MINOR`, the accounts
          ' state, the query string or the code version (IDM-R08). The pr
          esenter builds every new body, so the `extra-response-member` s
          eam (plan 000 section 8) changes new responses only. Account cr
          eation with a key runs the same steps with no lookup or lock (p
          lan 001 section 3.1).

      167  A rejection at step 6 or later that section 1.3 of spec 005 st
           ores (404 at lookup, 409 or 422 by a business rule, an `Alread
           yReversed` that the ledger adapter mapped from 23505, plan 002
            section 4) runs `ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT work`, then step 9 wit
           h the rejection's status, headers and body, then `COMMIT`. Eve
           ry other failure after step 3 runs `ROLLBACK`, so the key row 
           goes too (IDM-R17).

      54  | 3c  | Nothing updated: `SELECT fingerprint, status, headers, 
          body FROM idempotency_keys WHERE user_id = $user AND key = $key
          `, without a lock                                              
                                                                         
                               | Same fingerprint: `ROLLBACK`, replay the
           stored response (IDM-R07). Other fingerprint: `ROLLBACK`, 422 
          `idempotency-key-reused` (IDM-R09). Retries of a finished key r
          ead the row without locking it, so they never queue behind each
           other. No row (the cleanup deleted the expired row between ste
          p 3 and step 3b): go back to step 3, with `lock_timeout` set to
           the time left before the key-wait deadline, until the request 
          claims the key or finds a row, and then go on as above; a claim
           makes it a first request (IDM-R21). The loop ends: a pass find
          s no row only after a concurrent deletion of an expired row, th
          e cleanup deletes only expired rows, and a row inserted or repl
          aced since then has not expired, so the next pass either insert
          s the key or finds a live row, which 3c then reads. Every pass 
          stays within the key-wait deadline of step 2. |

      18  | `src/platform/logging/logger.ts`               | 08, 09      
           | 08-api: JSON lines with `level`, `time`, `msg` and `reqId` (
          SEC-R21). 09-hardening: pino `redact` for `authorization`, `coo
          kie` and `idempotency-key`, a request serializer that logs the 
          route template or path without the query string, and secrets ke
          pt out of startup errors (SEC-R22, SEC-R46).                   
                                                                         
                                                                         
                                                                         
                                                                         
                                                                         
                                                                         
                                                                         
                                                |
      19  | `src/platform/health/health.ts`                | 09-hardening
           | `/health/live` and `/health/ready`; readiness on a dedicated
           `pg` client outside the pool, `SELECT 1` within 1000 ms, and e
          very shipped migration present in `pgmigrations`; 503 during sh
          utdown (SEC-R23, SEC-R24, SEC-R26). The shipped migrations are 
          the `.sql` files of the folder found by `src/platform/db/migrat
          ions-dir.ts`, shared with `migrate.ts`: starting from its own d
          irectory, it walks up to the first ancestor that holds a `migra
          tions/` folder. From the source tree (`src/platform/db/`) that 
          is the repository's `migrations/`; from the build (`dist/platfo
          rm/db/`) it is `dist/migrations/`, which `npm run build` fills 
          by copying `migrations/*.sql` from 09-hardening on. It fails at
           startup if no folder or no migration is found, so readiness is
           never checked against an empty list. |
      20  | `src/platform/lifecycle/shutdown.ts`           | 09-hardening
           | The shutdown coordinator with an injected clock, driven by `
          close-with-grace`: drain delay, stop accepting, close idle keep
          -alive connections, wait for requests up to `SHUTDOWN_TIMEOUT_M
          S`, then close the pool, the readiness connection and Redis, in

⏺ All five findings from the third audit are fixed. npm run trace exits 0 and
  now lists 220 ACs, with the new SEC-AC37 pending. Prettier is clean, every
  spec is still Approved, and nothing is committed.

  1. Waiting for the key (plans 005 and 000): step 2 starts one deadline for the
     key wait. Before step 3b and before every loop back to step 3, the runner
     sets app.set_lock_timeout to the time left, and answers 409
     request-in-progress once none is left. One attempt now waits for the key at
     most IDEMPOTENCY_WAIT_TIMEOUT_MS in total. A new 07-idempotency task tests
     the double wait at steps 3 and 3b: it checks the request gives up within
     2000 ms and that the timeout set before step 3b is the time left.
  2. Request timeout (plan 007 §4, new ADR-0022):
     - At REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS the service answers 503 at once and never waits for
       the database first.
     - After the answer, if a statement is running, the runner cancels it
       through the readiness connection, using the database process ID (pid)
       read inside that transaction. It destroys the connection if the cancel
       call hasn't returned within 1000 ms; otherwise it waits for the
       statement, sends ROLLBACK and releases the connection.
     - A COMMIT already sent is awaited and never cancelled, and the answer
       stays 503. The committed-response branch is gone.
     - ADR-0022, "Request timeout: answer first, then cancel by the
       transaction's backend pid", records the mechanism and is linked from spec
       007 and the ADR index.
     - The SEC-AC25 task now asserts only what SEC-AC25 says. The extra checks
       are a separate task named SEC-R33.
  3. New requirement and AC: spec 007 now has SEC-R48 and SEC-AC37 (integration)
     for app.set_statement_timeout. Your approval is recorded in plan 000 §11.
     SEC-AC37 is named in the 05-schema session-functions task.
     - SEC-AC37 covers only the database side: the 1 to 3600000 ms range, that
       the value lasts only for its transaction, and that a role without the
       grant can't run it. That's what can be proven in 05-schema.
     - The script side of SEC-R48 (only the reconcile and cleanup scripts call
       it, with 600000) is proven under SEC-R48. That comes from the captured
       statements of LED-AC16 and IDM-AC24, and from item 4.
  4. Toolchain test (06-domain): it fails if app.set_statement_timeout appears
     anywhere in src/ outside ledger/adapters/cli/reconcile.ts and
     idempotency/adapters/cli/cleanup.ts.
  5. Auth tests (plan 006): in 08-api step 1 the verifier tests carry
     requirement IDs AUT-R02 to AUT-R05. A new step 6 task extends them to
     AUT-AC01, AUT-AC03, AUT-AC04 and AUT-AC05 once the HTTP error mapping
     (toProblem) exists, as SYS-AC16 does. AUT-AC01 doesn't strictly need that
     mapping, but I moved it with the others as you asked.

✻ Worked for 2m 2s · done 10:15 AM

❯ /audit                                                                        
  ⎿  I found 7 problems, one of them High: the plans build a request-timeout behaviour that approved spec 007 forbids. This phase is plans, tasks, two new ADRs and spec edits only, with no code yet, so the findings are about design and documents.

     High

     1. A COMMIT already in flight at the request deadline is answered 503 but still commits, which spec 007 rules out.
     - Where: specs/007-security-ops/plan.md:56, docs/adr/0022-request-timeout-answer-first-then-cancel-by-transaction-pid.md:26, specs/007-security-ops/tasks.md:47. They conflict with specs/007-security-ops/spec.md:167 (SEC-R33), :498 and :507.
     - What is wrong: SEC-R33 says a timed-out request has its transaction rolled back. The error catalogue (line 498) says "the transaction rolls back", and the invariant at line 507 says no timeout "leaves a committed effect". The plan and ADR-0022 do the opposite: they await the in-flight COMMIT and keep the 503. The task at line 47 tests that behaviour under SEC-R33. AGENTS.md says the spec wins and that no behaviour may exist without an AC.
     - How to trigger it: REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS (25 s) is reached while COMMIT is running. This can happen when slow WAL flushes or the deferred ledger and key triggers stretch the commit, or when a slow attempt 3 sends its COMMIT near the deadline.
     - Impact: a withdrawal, transfer or reversal takes effect while the client is told 503 "service unavailable". A client that retries with the same key gets the 201 by replay; one that does not retry believes the money did not move. Account creation without a key (ACC-R04, IDM-AC04) followed by a retry creates a second account.
     - Suggested fix: ask the owner to choose one of these.
       - (a) Amend SEC-R33, the catalogue row and the invariant to state the commit-in-flight exception, and add an AC for it.
       - (b) Change the runner so it never sends COMMIT after a cutoff (deadline minus a margin), and answers the real outcome when COMMIT was sent before the deadline. The margin must keep the answer under the load balancer's timeout.
     - AC: SEC-R33 / SEC-AC25; missing AC for the commit case.

     Medium

     2. The SEC-R48 requirement row is split into extra table cells, so half of the requirement disappears when the spec is rendered.
     - Where: specs/007-security-ops/spec.md:182
     - What is wrong: the || inside set_config('statement_timeout', ms || 'ms', true) was read as cell separators, and Prettier padded them into ms   |     | 'ms', true). The row now has 4 cells under a 2-column header. Markdown renderers drop the extra cells, so the row stops at "…runs `set_config('statement_timeout', ms".
     - How to trigger it: open spec 007 on GitHub or in any Markdown preview.
     - Impact: the security-relevant half of the requirement is lost: the value lasts only for the transaction, only the runtime role may execute the function, and only the reconcile and cleanup scripts may call it, with 600000. npm run trace does not parse requirement rows, so nothing catches this.
     - Suggested fix: rewrite the row as SEC-R31 does (set_config('statement_timeout', <ms>ms, true)), or escape the pipes as \|\|, then re-run Prettier.
     - AC: SEC-R48.

     Low

     3. The request-timeout clean-up does not define the state "transaction open, backend pid not yet known".
     - Where: specs/007-security-ops/plan.md:53-55, specs/000-overview/plan.md:146
     - What is wrong: the pid is learned only in the first statement after BEGIN. The clean-up rules cover "statement in flight with a pid", "COMMIT in flight" and "no transaction open". They say nothing about a deadline that fires while BEGIN or that first statement is running. An implementation that treats "no pid" as "no transaction" would release a connection with a transaction still open.
     - How to trigger it: a slow earlier attempt pushes the BEGIN of attempt 3 up to the deadline. Statement time is not in the SEC-R35 budget, so this can happen.
     - Impact: the next request on that pooled connection runs inside the leftover transaction (Postgres only warns on the second BEGIN).
     - Suggested fix: track "transaction open" separately from the pid. With no pid, await the in-flight statement, then ROLLBACK and release. Add that case to the SEC-R33 unit test at tasks.md:47.
     - AC: SEC-R33.

     4. The 1000 ms fallback in ADR-0022 brings back the problem the ADR uses to reject Option C.
     - Where: docs/adr/0022-request-timeout-answer-first-then-cancel-by-transaction-pid.md:25, specs/007-security-ops/plan.md:55-57
     - What is wrong: when pg_cancel_backend does not return within 1000 ms, the runner destroys the client connection. The ADR's own reason for rejecting Option C is that this leaves the backend statement running and holding its locks. The late cancel also stays queued on the single readiness connection, after the guarantee that the release waits for it no longer holds. Cancels and SELECT 1 share that connection, so a burst of timeouts can make readiness miss its 1000 ms limit.
     - How to trigger it: several requests time out together while the database is slow.
     - Impact:
       - Locks are held for up to statement_timeout (5 s) after a 503.
       - A healthy replica can be marked unready during a burst of timeouts.
     - Suggested fix: state in SEC-R33 that up to 5 s of lingering is accepted, or use a dedicated cancel connection apart from readiness. Also record the queued late cancel in the 12-infra follow-up.
     - AC: SEC-R33, SEC-R24.

     5. A test task relies on timing and asserts an exact bound it cannot meet.
     - Where: specs/005-idempotency/tasks.md:24
     - What is wrong: it sets up a wait of "about 1500 ms" and asserts the timeout ends "no later than 2000 ms after step 2". The deadline is taken on the client at step 2, while lock_timeout counts on the server from the start of each wait. The measured time therefore includes round trips and will go a few milliseconds past 2000.
     - How to trigger it: run the test on a loaded CI runner.
     - Impact: the test is flaky or fails consistently.
     - Suggested fix: assert on what is deterministic: the lock_timeout read before step 3b is the time left, below 2000. Bound the total with a margin (for example under 2000 ms plus 250 ms, and well under 2 × 2000 ms).
     - AC: IDM-R11 and IDM-R12 (building-block test).

     6. Plans 000 and 005 number the key step differently.
     - Where: specs/000-overview/plan.md:157-158, 176-177 against specs/005-idempotency/plan.md:52-54
     - What is wrong: plan 000 puts the replacement UPDATE inside step 3 and the read in step 3a. Plan 005 calls them 3b and 3c. The 55P03 classification ("step 3" means IdempotencyWaitTimeout) is defined by step number, and kysely-key-store.ts must tell the steps apart.
     - How to trigger it: an implementer follows the table in plan 000.
     - Impact: a 55P03 at the replacement or a re-pass could be classified wrongly as a 503 instead of a 409.
     - Suggested fix: use plan 005's numbering in plan 000, and list 3, 3b and the re-passes as key-wait steps.
     - AC: IDM-R12, IDM-R13.

     7. ADR-0022 lists only spec 007, but it changes behaviour in other specs.
     - Where: docs/adr/0022-request-timeout-answer-first-then-cancel-by-transaction-pid.md:5
     - What is wrong: it changes the runner in plan 000 (every read runs in a transaction, the pid is read per transaction) and the outcome of account creation without a key on timeout (specs 001 and 005). The ADR index rule says each ADR names every spec it affects, and each of those specs lists it.
     - Impact: the cross-references are incomplete, so readers of specs 000, 001 and 005 will not find this decision.
     - Suggested fix: add 000-overview, 001-accounts and 005-idempotency to "Related specs", and ADR-0022 to the "Related ADRs" lines of those specs. This metadata can be changed without asking.
     - AC: missing AC (metadata).

     Deferred, already planned: the RDS Proxy pinning and cross-connection pg_cancel_backend re-check (12-infra, follow-ups of ADR-0019 and ADR-0022); all code and tests (phases 05 to 11); the OpenAPI, README, runbook and AGENTS.md updates (doc tasks in 05 to 12); the e2e ACs (11-e2e); the ci ACs SEC-AC35 and DEP-AC15 to DEP-AC23 and DEP-AC26 (12-infra).

     What checked out:
     - Every AC in every spec appears in its plan's table, and a task names it in the same phase. The 12-infra extensions of SYS-AC14, SYS-AC17, SEC-AC29 and SEC-AC36 are planned on purpose.
     - The arithmetic in the edited SYS-AC12 is right (A1 ends at "1000" EUR).
     - The LED-AC15 timeouts satisfy the SEC-R35 budget (28130 < 30000).

     By category:
     - Spec conformance: finding 1.
     - Money: no findings. Amounts stay bigint and numeric sums, never number.
     - Transactions and concurrency: no findings. Locks are taken one by one in ascending order on customer accounts only, the checks run after the locks, and retries are bounded.
     - Idempotency: covered by finding 1. The key row is the first write, rejections are stored, and the wait timeout (409) and lock timeout (503) are separate settings.
     - Security: no findings in the planned code paths. Finding 2 is the spec defect on a security control.
     - Failure handling: findings 3 and 4.
     - Tests: finding 5.
     - Docs drift: findings 2, 6 and 7.

     Verdict: fix before shipping

❯ Fix all seven findings. For finding 1 I choose option (a), as the owner:      
  1. Amend spec 007, keeping it Approved, and record my approval in plan 000    
  section 11. SEC-R33: at REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS the service answers 503            
  /problems/service-unavailable with Retry-After: 1 at once, then cancels the   
  running statement and rolls back the transaction; IF the transaction's COMMIT 
  had already been sent, it lets the commit finish, so the outcome is unknown   
  to the client, as for the load balancer's gateway errors (DEP-R16), and a     
  retry with the same Idempotency-Key gets the stored response. Update the      
  error catalogue row and the invariant to say the same. Add a unit-level AC    
  for the commit-in-flight case (503 at the deadline, no ROLLBACK sent, the     
  commit completes), planned in 09-hardening. Say in section 1.9 that a         
  statement whose cancel cannot be delivered within 1000 ms may run until       
  statement_timeout after the 503. Align ADR-0022 and plan 007 with this text.  
  2. Rewrite SEC-R48 without the || expression, as SEC-R31 is written, so the   
  row has two cells, and check that no other requirement row has a pipe inside  
  it.                                                                           
  3. Plan 007: track "transaction open" apart from the pid; with a transaction  
  open and no pid yet, await the in-flight statement, then ROLLBACK and         
  release. Add the case to the SEC-R33 unit test.                               
  4. Record in ADR-0022 and in the 12-infra follow-up that a late cancel stays  
  queued on the readiness connection, and that a statement may run until        
  statement_timeout after a 503 when its cancel is not delivered.               
  5. specs/005-idempotency/tasks.md: assert that the lock_timeout set before    
  step 3b is the time left, below 2000 ms, and bound the total wait below 2000  
  ms plus 250 ms; never assert an exact 2000.                                   
  6. Plan 000: use plan 005's numbering (3, 3b, 3c and the re-passes), and      
  classify 55P03 at any of them as IdempotencyWaitTimeout.                      
  7. Add 000-overview, 001-accounts and 005-idempotency to ADR-0022's Related   
  specs, and ADR-0022 to those specs' Related ADRs lines.                       
     Change nothing else. Run npm run trace.

  ⎿  Updated specs/007-security-ops/spec.md (+13 -5)
      125  
      126  - **Role settings.** A migration run by the owner role sets `s
           tatement_timeout` and `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout` wi
           th `ALTER ROLE <runtime role> SET ...`, so local, CI and AWS g
           et the same values; the owner role is granted the right to alt
           er the runtime role. `statement_timeout` (5 s) is above both l
           ock waits, so a lock wait always ends as SQLSTATE 55P03, never
            57014; `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout` (10 s) ends a tr
           ansaction left open by a defect. The reconcile and cleanup scr
           ipts raise `statement_timeout` to 600000 ms (10 minutes) for t
           heir own transaction by calling `app.set_statement_timeout(ms 
           integer)`, a function of the same kind as the lock-timeout fun
           ction that accepts 1 to 3600000 ms (SEC-R29).
      127  - **Lock-timeout function.** `app.set_lock_timeout(ms integer)
           `, `SECURITY INVOKER`, checks 1 ≤ ms ≤ 60000 and runs `set_con
           fig('lock_timeout', ms || 'ms', true)`; only the runtime role 
           may execute it (SEC-R31). That RDS Proxy pins a connection on 
           `SET` and `set_config` statements but not on function calls is
            taken from its documentation and recorded in [ADR-0019](../..
           /docs/adr/0019-timeout-layers-and-rds-proxy.md), to be re-chec
           ked when RDS Proxy is introduced in phase 12-infra.
      128 -- **Statement timeout.** A statement cancelled by `statement_t
          -imeout` is a transient overload like a lock timeout, and the r
          -equest has already used most of its time, so it is answered 50
          -3 and not retried in process (SEC-R32).                       
      128 +- **Statement timeout.** A statement cancelled by `statement_t
          +imeout` is a transient overload like a lock timeout, and the r
          +equest has already used most of its time, so it is answered 50
          +3 and not retried in process (SEC-R32). At the request timeout
          + the 503 is answered first and the running statement is cancel
          +led afterwards (SEC-R33); a statement whose cancel cannot be d
          +elivered within 1000 ms may run until `statement_timeout` afte
          +r the 503.                                                    
      129  - **Pool size.** Each replica holds `DB_POOL_MAX` (default 10)
            request connections plus one readiness connection, and 10 con
           nections are kept in reserve for migrations, the reconcile and
            cleanup scripts and manual sessions (SEC-R36). Locally that i
           s 2 × 11 + 10 = 32 of PostgreSQL's 97 usable connections; in A
           WS the same rule applies to RDS Proxy's connection budget and 
           the maximum task count.

      164  | SEC-R30 | THE SYSTEM SHALL never send a `SET`, `SET LOCAL`, 
           `RESET` or `DISCARD` statement, a direct call of `set_config`,
            or a connection `options` parameter from the service, so that
            no session-level setting pins a connection behind RDS Proxy. 
                                                                         
                                                                         
                                                                         
                                                                         
                                             |

       7  ## Context and problem
       8  
       9 -SEC-R33 says that a request still being handled `REQUEST_TIMEOU
         -T_MS` after it was received gets a 503 with `Retry-After: 1`, a
         -nd that its running statement is cancelled and its database tra
         -nsaction rolled back. ADR-0019 orders the layers so the service
         - answers before the load balancer gives up. Two questions follo
         -w. First, how the service cancels a statement that runs on a po
         -oled connection, when `pg` has no cancel call on a pooled clien
         -t and the pool may be exhausted. Second, when it answers: befor
         -e or after the database has acknowledged the cancel. In AWS, RD
         -S Proxy multiplexes client connections over database backends b
         -etween transactions (ADR-0019), so a backend pid read once per 
         -connection can belong to another client by the time it is cance
         -lled. The owner decided the mechanism on 2026-10-08, after an a
         -udit showed that waiting for the database before answering can 
         -push the answer past the load balancer's timeout.              
       9 +SEC-R33 says that a request still being handled `REQUEST_TIMEOU
         +T_MS` after it was received gets a 503 with `Retry-After: 1` at
         + once, and that its running statement is then cancelled and its
         + database transaction rolled back, unless its `COMMIT` had alre
         +ady been sent, which then finishes. ADR-0019 orders the layers 
         +so the service answers before the load balancer gives up. Two q
         +uestions follow. First, how the service cancels a statement tha
         +t runs on a pooled connection, when `pg` has no cancel call on 
         +a pooled client and the pool may be exhausted. Second, when it 
         +answers: before or after the database has acknowledged the canc
         +el. In AWS, RDS Proxy multiplexes client connections over datab
         +ase backends between transactions (ADR-0019), so a backend pid 
         +read once per connection can belong to another client by the ti
         +me it is cancelled. The owner decided the mechanism on 2026-10-
         +08, after an audit showed that waiting for the database before 
         +answering can push the answer past the load balancer's timeout.
      10  
      11  ## Decision drivers
      12  
     ...
      23  Every database transaction reads `pg_backend_pid()` in its firs
          t round trip after `BEGIN`, combined with its first query where
           possible, and the runner drops the pid when the transaction en
          ds. At the deadline the runner sends nothing more on the reques
          t's connection and answers 503 at once. Then, after the answer,
           it cleans up:
      24  
      25  - If a statement other than `COMMIT` is in flight, it sends `SE
          LECT pg_cancel_backend($pid)` on the readiness connection and a
          waits its result. If that call has not returned within 1000 ms,
           it destroys the request's connection instead of reusing it. Ot
          herwise it awaits the in-flight statement whatever its outcome,
           sends `ROLLBACK` and releases the connection.
      26 -- If `COMMIT` is already in flight, it never cancels: it awaits
         - the commit and releases the connection. The answer stays 503, 
         -as for a response lost after a commit (IDM-AC21), and a keyed r
         -equest that is retried replays its stored response.            
      26 +- If a transaction is open but its pid is not known yet, it sen
         +ds no cancel: it awaits the in-flight statement, sends `ROLLBAC
         +K` and releases the connection.                                
      27 +- If `COMMIT` is already in flight, it never cancels and sends 
         +no `ROLLBACK`: it lets the commit finish and releases the conne
         +ction. The answer stays 503 and the outcome is unknown to the c
         +lient, as for the load balancer's gateway errors (DEP-R16); a r
         +etry with the same `Idempotency-Key` gets the stored response (
         +SEC-R33, SEC-AC38).                                            
      28  
      29  - **Pros:**
      30    - The answer time is the deadline itself, whatever the databa
          se does, so the timeout budget of SEC-R35 and the order of SEC-
          R34 hold.
     ...
      33    - A function call and a plain `SELECT` do not pin a connectio
          n (ADR-0019).
      34  - **Cons:**
      35    - One extra round trip per transaction when the pid cannot be
           read together with the first query.
      35 -  - The readiness connection now carries cancels too; a busy re
         -adiness check delays them, which the 1000 ms bound and the dest
         -royed connection absorb.                                       
      36 +  - The readiness connection now carries cancels too; a busy re
         +adiness check delays them, which the 1000 ms bound and the dest
         +royed connection absorb. A cancel that misses that bound stays 
         +queued on the readiness connection and can delay a readiness ch
         +eck, and the statement it was meant to stop may run, holding it
         +s locks, until `statement_timeout` after the 503, which section
         + 1.9 of spec 007 accepts.                                      
      37    - A request whose `COMMIT` lands after the deadline answered 
          503 although it committed; a keyless request, such as an accoun
          t creation without a key, cannot learn that, so a client that r
          etries it may create a second account.
      38    - The clean-up outlives the answer, so the connection returns
           to the pool up to the duration of the in-flight statement (bou
          nded by `statement_timeout`) or 1000 ms after it.
      39  
     ...
      57  
      58  ## Decision
      59  
      59 -Chosen option: **Option A**. The service answers 503 at `REQUES
         -T_TIMEOUT_MS`, as SEC-R33 says, and never waits for the databas
         -e before answering, so the layer order of SEC-R34 and ADR-0019 
         -holds. It cancels by the backend pid read inside the current tr
         -ansaction, through the readiness connection, and releases the c
         -onnection only after the cancel's result, so a cancel never rea
         -ches another request even behind RDS Proxy. A `COMMIT` in fligh
         -t is awaited, never cancelled, and the answer stays 503, as for
         - any response lost after a commit; keyed requests replay on ret
         -ry. Waiting for the database before answering (Option B) can pu
         -sh the answer past the load balancer's timeout; a pid per conne
         -ction or a destroyed socket alone (Option C) can cancel the wro
         -ng request or leave the statement running.                     
      60 +Chosen option: **Option A**. The service answers 503 at `REQUES
         +T_TIMEOUT_MS`, as SEC-R33 says, and never waits for the databas
         +e before answering, so the layer order of SEC-R34 and ADR-0019 
         +holds. It cancels by the backend pid read inside the current tr
         +ansaction, through the readiness connection, and releases the c
         +onnection only after the cancel's result, so a cancel never rea
         +ches another request even behind RDS Proxy. A `COMMIT` already 
         +sent is never cancelled or rolled back: it finishes, the answer
         + stays 503 and the outcome is unknown to the client, as SEC-R33
         + says, like the load balancer's gateway errors; keyed requests 
         +get the stored response on retry. A statement whose cancel cann
         +ot be delivered within 1000 ms may run until `statement_timeout
         +` after the 503 (section 1.9 of spec 007). Waiting for the data
         +base before answering (Option B) can push the answer past the l
         +oad balancer's timeout; a pid per connection or a destroyed soc
         +ket alone (Option C) can cancel the wrong request or leave the 
         +statement running.

      8  ## 1. Context and goal
  ⎿  … 4 more files changed

⏺ All seven findings from the fourth audit are fixed. npm run trace exits 0 and
  now lists 221 ACs, with the new SEC-AC38 pending. Prettier is clean, every
  spec is still Approved, and nothing is committed.

  1. Commit in flight at the request timeout (spec 007, option a):
     - SEC-R33: the service now answers 503 at once, then cancels the running
       statement and rolls back. If the COMMIT had already been sent, it
       finishes: the outcome is unknown to the client, as with gateway errors
       (DEP-R16), and a retry with the same key gets the stored response.
     - Catalogue row and invariant: both now say the same.
     - Section 1.9: it now says a statement whose cancel can't be delivered
       within 1000 ms may run until statement_timeout after the 503.
     - New unit AC, SEC-AC38: at the deadline the client gets 503, no cancel or
       ROLLBACK is sent, and the commit completes. It is planned and tasked in
       09-hardening.
     - Your approval is recorded in plan 000 §11, and plan 007 §4 and ADR-0022
       now match the spec.
  2. SEC-R48: the row now uses set_config('statement_timeout', <ms>ms, true), as
     SEC-R31 does, so it has two cells again. I checked every requirement row
     in all nine specs: each now has exactly two cells.
  3. Transaction open, pid not yet known (plan 007): the runner now tracks
     "transaction open" separately from the pid. If the deadline falls before
     the pid is known, it sends no cancel: it waits for the running statement,
     then sends ROLLBACK and releases the connection. The SEC-R33 unit-test task
     covers this case.
  4. Late cancels: ADR-0022 and its 12-infra follow-up record that a cancel
     which misses the 1000 ms bound stays queued on the readiness connection.
     They also record that the statement may then run until statement_timeout
     after the 503.
  5. Key-wait test (005 tasks): it asserts that the lock_timeout set before step
     3b is the time left, below 2000 ms, and that the total wait stays below
     2000 ms plus 250 ms. It never asserts an exact 2000.
  6. Step numbering (plan 000): the skeleton now uses plan 005's numbering: step
     3, step 3b, step 3c and the re-passes. A 55P03 at any of them is
     IdempotencyWaitTimeout (409); after them it is AccountLockTimeout (503).
  7. ADR-0022 cross-references: its Related specs now list 000, 001, 005 and
     007, and specs 000, 001 and 005 list ADR-0022 in their Related ADRs.

  ADR-0022 is marked Accepted, and I edited it in place as you asked. It is new
  on this uncommitted branch, so the rule against editing accepted ADRs applies
  to it only once it is merged.

✻ Worked for 1m 28s · done 10:25 AM

❯ /audit                                                                        
  ⎿  I found 2 Medium and 5 Low findings. The phase contains only specs, plans, tasks and ADRs (no code yet), so all of them are in the design. Every acceptance criterion of specs 000 to 008 has a phase and a test file in its plan and a task.

     Medium

     1. The amended request-timeout rule contradicts spec 005 and spec 000 on what a 503 means for idempotency
     - Where:
       - specs/005-idempotency/spec.md:92 (IDM-R17) and :56 (section 1.3 table)
       - specs/000-overview/spec.md:342 (the "No" note under the error catalogue) and :334
       - specs/005-idempotency/plan.md:64
     - What is wrong: SEC-R33 now lets a COMMIT that was already sent finish after the 503. The key row and the 201 are then stored. IDM-R17 still says every 503 rolls back the whole transaction, key row included, so a retry "executes the request again". Spec 000 says a "No" in the "Stored" column "means nothing was committed, so a retry with the same Idempotency-Key runs the request again", and its request-timeout row says "no". Plan 005 line 64 repeats the old rule ("a 503 (… request timeout) … ROLLBACK, so the key row goes too and a retry runs again").
     - How to trigger: a withdrawal whose COMMIT is sent at 900 ms with REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS 1000 (the SEC-AC38 scenario). The client gets a 503, but the money has moved.
     - Impact: three documents give opposite answers about whether a 503 had an effect. An implementer following IDM-R17 and plan 005 could cancel or roll back the in-flight commit and break SEC-AC38. A client following spec 000 could treat a request-timeout 503 as "nothing happened". For an account creation without a key, that means a duplicate account.
     - Suggested fix: with the owner's approval:
       - add the exception to IDM-R17: "except a request-timeout 503 whose COMMIT was already sent (SEC-R33)";
       - mark the request-timeout row in spec 000 section 4 "unknown, as for a gateway error";
       - change plan 005 line 64 to match;
       - add specs 003 and 004 to ADR-0022's related specs, since withdrawals and transfers are affected.
     - AC: IDM-R17, SEC-R33 / SEC-AC38.

     2. A cancel that misses the 1000 ms bound stays queued and can later cancel another request's statement
     - Where: docs/adr/0022-request-timeout-answer-first-then-cancel-by-transaction-pid.md:25,31,36; specs/007-security-ops/plan.md:55.
     - What is wrong: when pg_cancel_backend does not return within 1000 ms, the runner destroys the request's connection, and the cancel stays queued on the readiness connection. Nothing withdraws it, so it runs later against that pid. The claim "the release waits for the cancel's result, so a late cancel never reaches a backend that already serves another request" holds only on the non-destroy path. The ADR's own decision driver, "A cancel never reaches a statement of another request", is not met.
     - How to trigger: make the readiness connection or the database slow for more than 1000 ms while a request times out. The queued cancel fires seconds later. By then, behind RDS Proxy, the backend can serve another client, and locally the pid can have been reused.
     - Impact: an unrelated request, possibly at its COMMIT, is cancelled and answered 503. Queued cancels also delay readiness checks, so under database slowness every replica can turn unready together.
     - Suggested fix: make the cancel conditional on the transaction's identity. Read pg_backend_pid() together with now() (the transaction start) in the first round trip, and cancel with SELECT pg_cancel_backend(pid) FROM pg_stat_activity WHERE pid = $1 AND xact_start = $2. Alternatively, send each cancel on a short-lived connection that is destroyed when the 1000 ms bound expires; that needs SEC-R36 to count it. Add a unit test where the cancel resolves after the bound.
     - AC: SEC-R33 (ADR-0022 decision driver); missing AC for the late cancel.

     Low

     3. SEC-AC37's task cannot pass before the next task is done
     - Where: specs/000-overview/tasks.md:11-12.
     - What is wrong: SEC-AC37 asserts that after the commit statement_timeout is "the role's 5s". That value is set by the runtime-role-settings migration, which is the next task in the list.
     - How to trigger: do the tasks in the order written. The SEC-AC37 test fails, so the task cannot be ticked.
     - Impact: an ordering defect in the task list; it blocks the phase 05 work at step 1.
     - Suggested fix: swap the two step-1 tasks.
     - AC: SEC-AC37.

     4. No rule for a ROLLBACK that fails before the connection is released
     - Where: specs/000-overview/plan.md:146; specs/007-security-ops/plan.md:55.
     - What is wrong: both plans say "runs ROLLBACK" or "sends ROLLBACK and releases the connection". Neither says what happens if the ROLLBACK itself fails, for example when a late-processed cancel signal or a socket error hits it.
     - How to trigger: a ROLLBACK that errors. The connection goes back to the pool with a transaction still open or aborted. The next request's BEGIN only warns, and its statements fail with 25P02 (answered 500) or run inside the leftover transaction.
     - Impact: a failure can spread to the next request on that connection; this breaks ADR-0022's driver "No transaction is left open on a pooled connection".
     - Suggested fix: state in plan 000 section 6.1 that any error in ROLLBACK, or an unknown connection state, destroys the client (release(err)) instead of returning it to the pool. Add a unit test for it.
     - AC: missing AC.

     5. Database clean-up after the 503 is not counted by the shutdown deadline
     - Where: specs/007-security-ops/spec.md:122; specs/007-security-ops/plan.md:59.
     - What is wrong: spec section 1.8 argues that exit code 1 "happens only if a request outlives its own timeout", because SHUTDOWN_TIMEOUT_MS ≥ REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS. Under ADR-0022, database work continues after the 503: a commit still in flight, the await on the cancelled statement, or up to 1000 ms waiting for the cancel. The shutdown coordinator counts only HTTP requests in flight.
     - How to trigger: set SHUTDOWN_TIMEOUT_MS equal to REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS, which the configuration allows, and have a request time out near the shutdown deadline. The pool is closed or destroyed while that request's COMMIT or ROLLBACK is still running.
     - Impact: a spurious exit 1, or a commit cut off mid-flight. Money stays correct (the server commits or not), but the outcome is unknown to the client.
     - Suggested fix: make the coordinator wait for these post-answer clean-ups as in-flight work. Either require SHUTDOWN_TIMEOUT_MS ≥ REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS + 1000 + margin, or reword section 1.8.
     - AC: SEC-R27, SEC-R28, SEC-R35.

     6. "Only the runtime role may execute" the session functions is not true
     - Where:
       - specs/000-overview/plan.md:93-95
       - specs/007-security-ops/spec.md:127,182
       - docs/adr/0018-two-database-roles.md:50
     - What is wrong: REVOKE ALL … FROM PUBLIC does not remove the function owner's implicit EXECUTE. scf_owner (and superusers) can still call app.set_lock_timeout and app.set_statement_timeout. SEC-AC37 only tests a fresh role R, so it never sees this.
     - How to trigger: as scf_owner, run SELECT app.set_statement_timeout(3600000). It succeeds.
     - Impact: the requirement text and ADR-0018 overstate the restriction. The practical risk is low, because the owner can SET anyway.
     - Suggested fix: either add REVOKE EXECUTE … FROM scf_owner in the migration and assert it in the session-functions test, or reword SEC-R31, SEC-R48 and ADR-0018 to "PUBLIC cannot execute".
     - AC: SEC-R31, SEC-R48.

     7. A key that expires during its own key-wait steps is not treated as expired
     - Where: specs/005-idempotency/plan.md:53-54.
     - What is wrong: step 3b compares expires_at <= now(), and now() is the transaction's start time. Step 3c then reads the row without checking expiry.
     - How to trigger: a key row whose expires_at falls between the transaction start and step 3b (a window of milliseconds, or up to the key wait when step 3 waited), retried with a different body. Step 3b does not replace it, and step 3c answers 422 idempotency-key-reused instead of treating it as a first request. With the same body, it replays an expired response.
     - Impact: a rare deviation from IDM-R21.
     - Suggested fix: use clock_timestamp() in step 3b, and have step 3c loop back when the row it reads has expired.
     - AC: IDM-R21, IDM-R09.

     Deferred, already planned: every implementation and test listed in the tasks for 05-schema to 12-infra; re-checking the RDS Proxy pinning and backend behaviour (ADR-0019 and ADR-0022, 12-infra); the runbooks, alarms, OpenAPI and README updates (08-api, 09-hardening and later); the WAF rule and the Terraform checks (12-infra).

     - Spec conformance: no findings. Every AC of specs 000–008 has a phase, a test file and a task, and the plans add no behaviour that no spec describes.
     - Money: no findings. The plans use bigint in the domain, decimal strings at the API, int8 and numeric read as strings, and numeric sums for the reconciliation.
     - Security: no findings beyond #6.
     - Tests: no findings beyond #3. The plans rule out fixed sleeps by waiting on pg_stat_activity and pg_blocking_pids, and use injected clocks.

     Verdict: fix before shipping

❯ Fix all seven findings. For findings 1 and 2 I choose a simpler               
  request-timeout design, as the owner: the cancel by pid caused every finding  
  of the last three rounds.                                                     
  A. Spec 007, kept Approved, with my approval recorded in plan 000 section 11. 
  SEC-R33 becomes: IF a request is still being handled REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS       
  after it was received THEN THE SYSTEM SHALL answer 503                        
  /problems/service-unavailable with Retry-After: 1 at once, start no further   
  statement for it, and roll back its database transaction as soon as the       
  statement in flight ends, which statement_timeout bounds; IF its COMMIT was   
  already sent, the commit finishes and the outcome is unknown to the client,   
  as for the load balancer's gateway errors (DEP-R16), and a retry with the     
  same Idempotency-Key gets the stored response. The service never cancels a    
  statement from another connection. Rewrite SEC-AC25 to match: the 503 at 1000 
  ms, the third statement runs to its end, the fourth and fifth never run,      
  then the rollback. Keep SEC-AC38. Update sections 1.8 and 1.9, the catalogue  
  row and the invariant.                                                        
  B. Spec 005 (IDM-R17 and the section 1.3 table) and spec 000 section 4 (the   
  request-timeout row and the note under the catalogue): a request-timeout 503  
  whose COMMIT was already sent has an unknown outcome, as a gateway error      
  does; every other 503 commits nothing. Make plan 005 match.                   
  C. ADR-0022 is not committed yet, so rewrite it for this design: answer       
  first, then roll back when the statement in flight ends, with no              
  cross-connection cancel, because a cancel by pid can reach another request's  
  statement behind RDS Proxy or after the pid is reused, while                  
  statement_timeout already bounds every statement to 5 s. Rename its file to   
  match, and add specs 003 and 004 to its Related specs, with the back-links.   
  D. Remove pg_backend_pid() and the cancel through the readiness connection    
  from plans 000, 001, 005 and 007; reads may run as single statements again.   
  E. The shutdown coordinator counts the clean-ups that run after a 503 as      
  in-flight work; reword spec 007 section 1.8 so that exit code 1 also covers a 
  clean-up cut off by SHUTDOWN_TIMEOUT_MS.                                      
  3. specs/000-overview/tasks.md: swap the two step-1 tasks, so the role        
  settings exist before the SEC-AC37 test.                                      
  4. Plan 000 section 6.1: an error in ROLLBACK, or any unknown connection      
  state, destroys the client (release with an error) instead of returning it to 
  the pool. Add a unit test.

  scf_owner, so only scf_app holds it, as SEC-R31, SEC-R48 and ADR-0018 say;    
  assert it in the session-functions test.                                      
  7. Plan 005: compare expiry with clock_timestamp() in step 3b, and loop back  
  from step 3c when the row it reads has expired.

      147  ### 6.1 Transaction runner
      148  
      146 -`runTransaction(work, { retry })`: it takes one pool connectio
          -n, keeps it for every attempt and releases it after the last o
          -ne, so the pool acquire timeout is waited at most once per req
          -uest, as SYS-R35 and SEC-R35 count it. Each attempt runs `BEGI
          -N ISOLATION LEVEL READ COMMITTED`, runs `work`, and `COMMIT`. 
          -On any error it runs `ROLLBACK`. With `retry: 'movement'`, an 
          -error with SQLSTATE 40P01 or 40001 (at any statement, `COMMIT`
          - included) starts a new attempt after `sleep(random() × bound(
          -n))`, `bound(n) = min(200, 10 × 2^(n−1))` ms, up to 3 attempts
          -; after the third it throws `RetriesExhausted` (SYS-R18, SYS-R
          -19). No other error is retried: 55P03 and 57014 are answered a
          -t once (MOV-R20, SEC-R32). Movements and reversals use `retry:
          - 'movement'`; account creation and status changes use `retry: 
          -'none'`, since SYS-R18 covers money movements only. Reads run 
          -in a READ COMMITTED transaction too, so that the request timeo
          -ut always knows which database backend to cancel (plan 007 sec
          -tion 4). Every attempt learns its backend pid inside the trans
          -action: the first statement returns `pg_backend_pid()` in the 
          -same round trip where it can (step 2 of section 6.2, the lock-
          -timeout call of a status change), and as a separate `SELECT pg
          -_backend_pid()` right after `BEGIN` otherwise. The pid is neve
          -r kept across transactions, because behind RDS Proxy a client 
          -connection can move to another backend between transactions (A
          -DR-0019).                                                     
      149 +`runTransaction(work, { retry })`: it takes one pool connectio
          +n, keeps it for every attempt and releases it after the last o
          +ne, so the pool acquire timeout is waited at most once per req
          +uest, as SYS-R35 and SEC-R35 count it. Each attempt runs `BEGI
          +N ISOLATION LEVEL READ COMMITTED`, runs `work`, and `COMMIT`. 
          +On any error it runs `ROLLBACK`. With `retry: 'movement'`, an 
          +error with SQLSTATE 40P01 or 40001 (at any statement, `COMMIT`
          + included) starts a new attempt after `sleep(random() × bound(
          +n))`, `bound(n) = min(200, 10 × 2^(n−1))` ms, up to 3 attempts
          +; after the third it throws `RetriesExhausted` (SYS-R18, SYS-R
          +19). No other error is retried: 55P03 and 57014 are answered a
          +t once (MOV-R20, SEC-R32). Movements and reversals use `retry:
          + 'movement'`; account creation and status changes use `retry: 
          +'none'`, since SYS-R18 covers money movements only. Reads run 
          +single statements outside an explicit transaction. If `ROLLBAC
          +K` fails, or the connection is in any state the runner cannot 
          +account for (a transaction possibly still open or aborted, a p
          +rotocol error), the runner releases the client with an error (
          +`release(err)`), so the pool destroys it instead of handing it
          + to another request.

      66  
      67  Steps 3, 3b and 3c share one wait budget: before step 3b and be
          fore every pass back to step 3, the runner calls `SELECT app.se
          t_lock_timeout($remaining)` with the whole milliseconds left un
          til the deadline of step 2, and answers `IdempotencyWaitTimeout
          ` (409 `request-in-progress`, `Retry-After: 1`) at once when le
          ss than 1 ms remains. `lock_timeout` bounds each wait, not thei
          r sum, so without this an attempt could wait for the key once a
          t step 3 and again at step 3b; with it, one attempt never waits
           for the key longer than `IDEMPOTENCY_WAIT_TIMEOUT_MS` in total
          , as SYS-R35 and SEC-R35 count it. A 55P03 at any of these step
          s is `IdempotencyWaitTimeout` (409, IDM-R12); one at any later 
          step is `AccountLockTimeout` (503, IDM-R13). `kysely-key-store.
          ts` tells them apart by the step at which the error was raised.

  ⎿  Deleted docs/adr/0022-request-timeout-answer-first-then-cancel-by-tran
     saction-pid.md (+0 -84)
       1 -# ADR-0022: Request timeout: answer first, then cancel by the t
         -ransaction's backend pid                                       
       2 -                                                               
       3 -- **Status:** Accepted                                         
       4 -- **Date:** 2026-10-08                                         
       5 -- **Related specs:** 000-overview, 001-accounts, 005-idempotenc
         -y, 007-security-ops                                            
       6 -                                                               
       7 -## Context and problem                                         
       8 -                                                               
       9 -SEC-R33 says that a request still being handled `REQUEST_TIMEOU
         -T_MS` after it was received gets a 503 with `Retry-After: 1` at
         - once, and that its running statement is then cancelled and its
         - database transaction rolled back, unless its `COMMIT` had alre
         -ady been sent, which then finishes. ADR-0019 orders the layers 
         -so the service answers before the load balancer gives up. Two q
         -uestions follow. First, how the service cancels a statement tha
         -t runs on a pooled connection, when `pg` has no cancel call on 
         -a pooled client and the pool may be exhausted. Second, when it 
         -answers: before or after the database has acknowledged the canc
         -el. In AWS, RDS Proxy multiplexes client connections over datab
         -ase backends between transactions (ADR-0019), so a backend pid 
         -read once per connection can belong to another client by the ti
         -me it is cancelled. The owner decided the mechanism on 2026-10-
         -08, after an audit showed that waiting for the database before 
         -answering can push the answer past the load balancer's timeout.
      10 -                                                               
      11 -## Decision drivers                                            
      12 -                                                               
      13 -- The answer arrives at `REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS`, never later, so t
         -he order database < service < load balancer of SEC-R34 holds.  
      14 -- A cancel never reaches a statement of another request.       
      15 -- No transaction is left open on a pooled connection after the 
         -request ends.                                                  
      16 -- No `SET`, and nothing that pins a connection behind RDS Proxy
         - (SEC-R30).                                                    
      17 -- It works when every pool connection is busy.                 
      18 -                                                               
      19 -## Considered options                                          
      20 -                                                               
      21 -### Option A: Answer at the deadline, then clean up with `pg_ca
         -ncel_backend` on the transaction's own pid, through the readine
         -ss connection                                                  
      22 -                                                               
      23 -Every database transaction reads `pg_backend_pid()` in its firs
         -t round trip after `BEGIN`, combined with its first query where
         - possible, and the runner drops the pid when the transaction en
         -ds. At the deadline the runner sends nothing more on the reques
         -t's connection and answers 503 at once. Then, after the answer,
         - it cleans up:                                                 
      24 -                                                               
      25 -- If a statement other than `COMMIT` is in flight, it sends `SE
         -LECT pg_cancel_backend($pid)` on the readiness connection and a
         -waits its result. If that call has not returned within 1000 ms,
         - it destroys the request's connection instead of reusing it. Ot
         -herwise it awaits the in-flight statement whatever its outcome,
         - sends `ROLLBACK` and releases the connection.                 
      26 -- If a transaction is open but its pid is not known yet, it sen
         -ds no cancel: it awaits the in-flight statement, sends `ROLLBAC
         -K` and releases the connection.                                
      27 -- If `COMMIT` is already in flight, it never cancels and sends 
         -no `ROLLBACK`: it lets the commit finish and releases the conne
         -ction. The answer stays 503 and the outcome is unknown to the c
         -lient, as for the load balancer's gateway errors (DEP-R16); a r
         -etry with the same `Idempotency-Key` gets the stored response (
         -SEC-R33, SEC-AC38).                                            
      28 -                                                               
      29 -- **Pros:**                                                    
      30 -  - The answer time is the deadline itself, whatever the databa
         -se does, so the timeout budget of SEC-R35 and the order of SEC-
         -R34 hold.                                                      
      31 -  - A pid read inside the transaction is the backend serving th
         -at transaction until it ends, because RDS Proxy keeps a client 
         -on one backend within a transaction; the release waits for the 
         -cancel's result, so a late cancel cannot hit the next client of
         - that backend.                                                 
      32 -  - The readiness connection is outside the request pool (SEC-R
         -24), so a cancel works even when the pool is exhausted.        
      33 -  - A function call and a plain `SELECT` do not pin a connectio
         -n (ADR-0019).                                                  
      34 -- **Cons:**                                                    
      35 -  - One extra round trip per transaction when the pid cannot be
         - read together with the first query.                           
      36 -  - The readiness connection now carries cancels too; a busy re
         -adiness check delays them, which the 1000 ms bound and the dest
         -royed connection absorb. A cancel that misses that bound stays 
         -queued on the readiness connection and can delay a readiness ch
         -eck, and the statement it was meant to stop may run, holding it
         -s locks, until `statement_timeout` after the 503, which section
         - 1.9 of spec 007 accepts.                                      
      37 -  - A request whose `COMMIT` lands after the deadline answered 
         -503 although it committed; a keyless request, such as an accoun
         -t creation without a key, cannot learn that, so a client that r
         -etries it may create a second account.                         
      38 -  - The clean-up outlives the answer, so the connection returns
         - to the pool up to the duration of the in-flight statement (bou
         -nded by `statement_timeout`) or 1000 ms after it.              
      39 -                                                               
      40 -### Option B: Wait for the database before answering

           ores (404 at lookup, 409 or 422 by a business rule, an `Alread
           yReversed` that the ledger adapter mapped from 23505, plan 002
            section 4) runs `ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT work`, then step 9 wit
