      8  ## 1. Context and goal
  ⎿  … 5 more files changed

  Searched for 1 pattern (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ All seven findings are fixed, using your simpler request-timeout design for
  findings 1 and 2. npm run trace exits 0 (221 ACs), Prettier is clean, every
  spec is still Approved, and every requirement row has two cells. Nothing is
  committed.

  A. Spec 007 (your approval is recorded in plan 000 §11):
  - SEC-R33: at REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS the service answers 503 at once and starts no
    further statement. It rolls back once the statement in flight ends, which
    statement_timeout bounds. A COMMIT already sent finishes with an outcome
    unknown to the client, as for a gateway error, and a retry with the same key
    gets the stored response. The service never cancels a statement from
    another connection.
  - SEC-AC25: now says the 503 comes at 1000 ms, the third statement runs to its
    end, the fourth and fifth never run, then the rollback. SEC-AC38 is
    unchanged.
  - Sections 1.8 and 1.9, the error catalogue row and the invariant: all updated
    to match. Section 1.8 now says exit code 1 also covers a clean-up cut off
    by SHUTDOWN_TIMEOUT_MS.

  B. Specs 005 and 000:
  - IDM-R17 now has the exception for a request-timeout 503 sent after its
    COMMIT. The table in spec 005 §1.3 splits the request timeout into before
    COMMIT (rolled back, not stored) and after COMMIT (outcome unknown, stored
    if it succeeds).
  - In spec 000 §4, the request-timeout row and the note under the catalogue say
    the same: that case's outcome is unknown, as for a gateway error, and every
    other 503 commits nothing.
  - Plan 005 §3 matches.

  C. ADR-0022 is rewritten for the new design and renamed to
  0022-request-timeout-answer-first-then-roll-back.md.
  - Chosen: answer first, start no further statement, roll back after the
    statement in flight ends.
  - Rejected:
    - a cancel by pid, because it can reach another request's statement, and
      statement_timeout already bounds every statement to 5 s;
    - waiting for the database before answering;
    - destroying the connection at the deadline.
  - It now also relates to specs 003 and 004. All links point to the new file:
    specs 000, 001, 003, 004, 005 and 007, plan 007, and the ADR index.

  D. No more backend pid or cancel: pg_backend_pid() and the cancel through the
  readiness connection are gone from plans 000, 001, 005 and 007. Reads and
  account creation without a key run as single statements again. The
  status-change and key-step statements call app.set_lock_timeout alone.

  E. Shutdown: the coordinator counts the clean-up of a request already answered
  503 as in-flight work, and destroys it at the deadline with exit 1. A new
  09-hardening task tests this.

  Your numbered items:
  - 3. The two step-1 tasks in spec 000 are swapped, so the role settings exist
    before the SEC-AC37 test.
  - 4. Plan 000 §6.1: a failed ROLLBACK, or any unknown connection state,
    releases the connection with an error so the pool destroys it. A new
    unit-test task covers this.
  - 6. The app-functions migration also revokes EXECUTE on both session
    functions from scf_owner, so scf_app is the only role that holds it. The
    session-functions task asserts this. A superuser can still call them.
  - 7. Plan 005 step 3b compares expiry with clock_timestamp() instead of the
    transaction start. Step 3c loops back when the row it reads has expired, and
    the explanation of why the loop ends covers that case. Plan 000 matches.

  The only remaining mention of pg_cancel_backend is in ADR-0022's context
  section, which explains why the first design was rejected.

✻ Cooked for 2m 40s · done 10:36 AM

❯ /audit                                                                        
  ⎿  I reviewed the phase/04-plans branch against main. It contains no code: just the 18 new plan and task files, two new ADRs (0021, 0022), and the edited specs, ADR index and dependency list. I read every changed file in full.

     Coverage is complete. Every acceptance criterion in every spec appears in its plan's table with the same level, and every one is named by at least one task. The traceability script rejects any AC ID written outside a task's checkbox line, and there are none. All eight findings are Low.

     Findings

     1. Low: "55P03 at the key insert" contradicts the plan's key-wait steps
     - Where: specs/005-idempotency/plan.md:90-91, specs/003-money-movements/plan.md:109, specs/004-reversals/plan.md:70
     - What is wrong: These error tables say the 409 (IdempotencyWaitTimeout) is for a 55P03 "at the key insert", and the 503 (AccountLockTimeout) for a 55P03 "after the key insert". But plan 005 section 3 and plan 000 section 6.3 say a 55P03 at any key-wait step (3, 3b, 3c or a re-pass) is the 409.
     - How to trigger: An implementer codes from the error tables. A request then waits at step 3b (the update that replaces an expired key) behind a concurrent replacement and times out.
     - Impact: The client gets 503 instead of 409, which breaks IDM-R12 and MOV-R29. The key-step test in specs/005-idempotency/tasks.md:24 would catch it, so the risk is small.
     - Fix: Write "55P03 at a key-wait step (3, 3b, 3c or a re-pass)" and "55P03 at an account lock" in all three tables.
     - AC: IDM-R12, MOV-R29 (IDM-AC13)

     2. Low: a 05-schema task uses a helper built three steps later
     - Where: specs/002-ledger/tasks.md:11 against specs/000-overview/plan.md:16-24 and specs/000-overview/tasks.md:15
     - What is wrong: The step 3 ledger-schema test runs "on a scratch database". The scratch-database helper in test/support/db.ts is only built in step 6.
     - How to trigger: Follow the cross-spec order as written.
     - Impact: The step 3 test cannot be written first, or it has to duplicate the helper.
     - Fix: Move the scratch-database part of test/support/db.ts to step 1 or 2, or move this test to step 7.
     - AC: missing AC (sequencing only)

     3. Low: two spec sentences no longer match ADR-0022
     - Where: specs/000-overview/spec.md:117 (SYS-R35) and specs/007-security-ops/spec.md:513 (invariant)
     - What is wrong: SYS-R35 says the request timeout "bounds the time spent running statements". The invariant says "statements before REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS". Under ADR-0022, a statement still running at the deadline keeps running, up to statement_timeout (5 s), after the 503.
     - How to trigger: A statement starts at 24.9 s with the default 25 s request timeout. It can run until 29.9 s, holding its locks and its connection.
     - Impact: Readers draw the wrong conclusion about how long pool connections and locks can be held.
     - Fix: Reword both: the request timeout bounds when the answer is sent; statement_timeout bounds the clean-up after it.
     - AC: SEC-R33, SYS-R35

     4. Low: ADR-0021 overstates what the function restriction protects
     - Where: docs/adr/0021-statement-timeout-function-for-maintenance-scripts.md:28-30, 44-45, 70
     - What is wrong: Any session of scf_app can run SET LOCAL statement_timeout or set_config directly, because the setting is changeable by any user. So restricting EXECUTE and capping the function at one hour do not stop the service raising its own limit. Option C's claimed advantage ("the service's role could never raise its limit") is also false: a role-level setting is only a default that a session can override.
     - How to trigger: Any code path in the service sends SET LOCAL statement_timeout = 0.
     - Impact: The decision record suggests a safety property that does not exist. The real guards are SEC-R30 and its statement-capture tests (SEC-AC22, SEC-AC23) plus the toolchain text check.
     - Fix: State that the function exists to avoid RDS Proxy connection pinning and to keep the call easy to review, not as a privilege boundary. Correct Option C.
     - AC: SEC-R48 (docs accuracy)

     5. Low: no test that the deadline stops a retry from starting
     - Where: specs/007-security-ops/tasks.md:47-49, specs/007-security-ops/plan.md:54-55
     - What is wrong: The request-timeout tests cover a statement in flight, a COMMIT in flight, a failed ROLLBACK and "no transaction open". None covers the deadline firing during the retry backoff sleep after a 40001 or 40P01, or a pool connection that is acquired only after the deadline.
     - How to trigger: A 40001 at attempt 1, and the deadline passes while the runner sleeps.
     - Impact: A second attempt could BEGIN and commit after the 503 was answered. That outcome is unknown to the client, and SEC-R33 only allows it for a COMMIT already sent.
     - Fix: Add a SEC-R33 unit case with an injected clock. Check that no BEGIN is sent after the deadline, and that a connection acquired late is released unused.
     - AC: SEC-R33 (SEC-AC25 does not cover this)

     6. Low: a timing upper bound that may flake in CI
     - Where: specs/005-idempotency/tasks.md:24
     - What is wrong: The test requires the total key wait from step 2 to stay below "2000 ms plus 250 ms". That is wall-clock time, including client round trips.
     - How to trigger: A loaded CI runner or a slow container.
     - Impact: The test can fail intermittently.
     - Fix: Make the deterministic checks the main assertions (each lock_timeout set before step 3b equals the time left, and their sum stays at or below 2000). Keep only a generous wall-clock limit, for example under two full waits (4000 ms).
     - AC: IDM-R11, IDM-R12

     7. Low: plan 000 still describes the superseded cancel-based design
     - Where: specs/000-overview/plan.md:265
     - What is wrong: Section 11 records approval of a SEC-R33 design that "cancels and rolls back", including "a statement whose cancel cannot be delivered within 1000 ms". It then says the design was replaced. The superseded design stays in the main plan file.
     - How to trigger: An implementer reads section 11 without the end of the paragraph.
     - Impact: They might implement pg_cancel_backend, which SEC-R33 now forbids ("never cancel a statement from another connection").
     - Fix: Keep one sentence for the approved design and refer to ADR-0022 for the history.
     - AC: SEC-R33

     8. Low: the wait for the statement in flight has no client-side limit
     - Where: specs/007-security-ops/plan.md:55, docs/adr/0022-request-timeout-answer-first-then-roll-back.md:25
     - What is wrong: After the 503, the runner waits for the statement in flight "at most until statement_timeout". That is a server-side limit. If the network to the database is cut, the reply never arrives and the wait never ends.
     - How to trigger: A network failure between the replica and the database (or RDS Proxy) while a statement runs.
     - Impact: Pool connections stay held until shutdown destroys them. Repeated failures empty the pool, and requests get 503 for pool acquire timeouts.
     - Fix: Add a client-side limit a little above statement_timeout, for example the pg query_timeout option or a runner timer. When it fires, release the client with an error so the pool destroys it.
     - AC: SEC-R33, SEC-R36

     Deferred, already planned: all code and tests from 05-schema to 12-infra; the OpenAPI, README and runbook updates assigned to each phase, including the runbook on 503 answers with an unknown outcome (12-infra); the RDS Proxy pinning re-check (12-infra, ADR-0019 and ADR-0021).

     Categories without findings

     - Spec conformance: no findings. Every AC is placed with the right level and phase, named by a task, and has no AC ID outside a task line. No behaviour is planned that no spec describes.
     - Money: no findings. Amounts are bigint in the domain and strings at the API. Sums use numeric, and the lint rule forbids number conversions.
     - Transactions and concurrency: no findings. Each movement is one transaction. Customer accounts are locked one by one in ascending order with kind = 'customer', so system accounts are never locked. Checks run after the locks, and retries are capped at 3 attempts with backoff.
     - Idempotency: only finding 1. Apart from that, the plans follow specs 003 and 005: the key row is the first write, replays return the stored bytes, a different body answers 422, business rejections are stored and unexpected errors are not, and the key wait (409) and account lock timeout (503) are separate settings.
     - Security: no issues in the planned implementation (finding 4 is about the ADR's wording). Roles are checked before ids, other customers' resources answer 404, schemas are strict, secrets are redacted, and SQL is parameterized.
     - Failure handling: findings 5 and 8 only.
     - Tests: findings 2, 5 and 6 only.
     - Docs drift: findings 3, 4 and 7 only. The ADR index and docs/dependencies.md match the plans.

     Follow-ups: 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8
     Verdict: ready to ship

     Critical Files for Implementation

     - /Users/matiastorsello/supercool-finances/specs/000-overview/plan.md
     - /Users/matiastorsello/supercool-finances/specs/005-idempotency/plan.md
     - /Users/matiastorsello/supercool-finances/specs/007-security-ops/plan.md
     - /Users/matiastorsello/supercool-finances/docs/adr/0022-request-timeout-answer-first-then-roll-back.md
     - /Users/matiastorsello/supercool-finances/docs/adr/0021-statement-timeout-function-for-maintenance-scripts.md

  3. I approve, as the owner, rewording SYS-R35 and the spec 007 invariant: the 
  request timeout bounds when the answer is sent, and statement_timeout bounds  
  the clean-up after it. Keep both specs Approved and record my approval in     
  plan 000 section 11.                                                          
  4. ADR-0021 is not committed yet: say the function exists to avoid RDS Proxy  
  pinning and to keep the call easy to review, not as a privilege boundary,     
  since any session can SET LOCAL statement_timeout; the real guards are        
  SEC-R30, its statement-capture tests and the toolchain text check. Correct

  5. Add a SEC-R33 unit case with an injected clock: when the deadline passes   
  during the retry backoff or while waiting for a pool connection, no BEGIN is  
  sent after the deadline, and a connection acquired late is released unused.   
  6. specs/005-idempotency/tasks.md: the main assertions are deterministic      
  (each lock_timeout set before step 3b equals the time left, and their sum     
  stays at or below 2000 ms); keep only a generous wall-clock limit, below 4000 
  ms.                                                                           
  7. Plan 000 section 11: keep one sentence for the approved SEC-R33 design and 
  refer to ADR-0022 for its history.                                            
  8. Plan 007 and ADR-0022: the wait for the statement in flight after a 503    
  gets a client-side limit a little above statement_timeout; when it fires,     
  release the client with an error so the pool destroys it.                     
     Change nothing else. Run npm run trace.

⏺ All eight follow-ups are fixed. npm run trace exits 0 (221 ACs), Prettier is
  clean and all nine specs are still Approved. Nothing is committed.

  1. Error tables (plans 003, 004, 005): the 409 is now "55P03 at a key-wait
     step (3, 3b, 3c or a re-pass)" and the 503 is "55P03 at an account lock".
     The key-store row in plan 005 matches.
  2. Scratch databases: a new step 1 task in spec 000 builds the
     scratch-database part of test/support/db.ts, with its own test, so the step
     3 ledger test can use it. The step 6 task now completes the rest of the
     file, and the 05-schema order in plan 000 §1 matches.
  3. SYS-R35 and the spec 007 invariant: both now say the request timeout bounds
     when the answer is sent, and statement_timeout bounds the clean-up after
     it. Your approval is recorded in plan 000 §11.
  4. ADR-0021: it now says the function exists to avoid RDS Proxy pinning and to
     keep the call easy to review, not as a privilege boundary, since any
     session can run SET LOCAL statement_timeout. The real guards are SEC-R30,
     its statement-capture tests (SEC-AC22, SEC-AC23) and the toolchain text
     check. Option C is corrected: a role-level setting is only a session
     default.
  5. Deadline before a transaction starts: a new SEC-R33 unit-test task with an
     injected clock covers a deadline during the retry backoff or while waiting
     for a pool connection. It checks the 503 comes at the deadline, no BEGIN is
     sent after it, and a connection acquired late is released unused. The same
     task also covers the client-side limit from item 8.
  6. Key-wait test (spec 005 tasks): the main assertions are now deterministic:
     each lock_timeout equals the time left, and their sum is at most 2000 ms.
     The only wall-clock limit is a generous one: the whole wait from step 2
     stays below 4000 ms.
  7. Plan 000 §11: the superseded cancel design is gone. One sentence records
     the approved SEC-R33 design and points to ADR-0022 for its history.
  8. Wait for the statement in flight (plan 007 §4 and ADR-0022): it has a
     client-side limit of statement_timeout + 1000 ms. When the limit fires, the
     connection is released with an error so the pool destroys it. The 1000 ms
     margin is my choice; you only asked for "a little above".

  skill or your explicit request, and I can't invoke /ship myself. It also
  exports this session's transcript and adds it to the AI usage log.
